Cooling Compressor
The cooling Compressor provides filtered air to the marking unit and provides a positive flow of air across the protective window
of the marking head, keeping the glass clean longer. Air flow is monitored at the LIC_ JPT board and if the flow is too low or too
high, the system will assert an error. In addition to air flow, orientation of the compressor assembly is also monitored at the LIC_
JPT board.
130
Rev AC

Supply Unit
The compressor is located near the front of the supply unit. It is powered by 24V from the Terminal Block. The Compressor uses
the same 24V power supply as the laser source and only runs when the laser source is switched on.
F IGURE 3- 44: Location of Compressor within supply unit
Compressor Maintenance
The expected life of the compressor is 16,000- 20,000 hours of run time. The input and output filters should be changed every 6
months under normal operating conditions. Additionally, you should blow out or replace the fan filters on the front of the supply
unit regularly to keep the system operating well.
131
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 3- 45: Location of filters and UADB board
The UADB board and the compressor mounting bracket are not included with a new compressor kit, so do not discard the old
compressor assembly.
UADB  Removed Starting December 2021
The UADB board and cable will no longer be used in 7340/7440 units manufactured after December 2021. If
UADB board fails in laser then CMark should be upgraded to version 4.12.1 or newer which no longer monitors
the UADB board. Spare parts are not available to replace UADB or UADB cable.
132
Rev AC

Supply Unit
Orientation
The compressor is fitted into a bracket that can be rotated in two directions. This is necessary as the compressor orientation
needs to be changed if the supply unit is to be mounted on its side. The UADB board detects the orientation of the compressor
and if the compressor not in the correct orientation, errors will assert in the user interface. This board will not be present in lasers
manufactured after December 2021.
The system cannot operate if the supply unit is mounted with the umbilical facing up or umbilical facing down. The pump cannot
operate in these orientations.
If compressor feet do not face down (horizontal) then life of compressor may be decreased.
F IGURE 3- 46: Approved supply unit orientations
There is an additional set of slotted holes for rotating the pump assembly to its side (see Figure 3- 47 below ) for use when mount -
ing the supply unit is on its side ( Figure 3- 48 on the next page ). UADB board will not be present on units manufactured after
December 2021.
F IGURE 3- 47: Red arrows show holes for mounting screws when supply unit installed on either side

Universal Acceleration Detection Board
(UADB)
UADB  Removed Starting December 2021
The UADB board and cable will no longer be used in 7340/7440 units manufactured after December 2021. If
UADB board fails in laser then CMark should be upgraded to version 4.12.1 or newer which no longer monitors
the UADB board. Spare parts are not available to replace UADB or UADB cable.
The Universal Acceleration Detection Board (UADB) is mounted to the compressor mounting bracket. The board detects the ori -
entation of the mounting bracket, and therefore the compressor as the compressor must be mounted in the correct orientation.
If the compressor is mounted incorrectly, the life of the pump cannot be guaranteed.
There UADB is similar in concept to components in cellular phones that sense what orientation the phone is in so that the screen
view can be rotated.
If the UADB and compressor are not mounted in the correct orientation then error "E00312: Wrong air pump assembly position"
will occur. See "Cooling Compressor" on page 130 for more information on changing the mounting position of the compressor.

the LIC_ JPT board. The board is available as a spare part if necessary and is not part of the compressor spare part kit.

Supply Unit
CP- Light 7340/7440
Located within the supply unit, the CP- Light circuit board contains the drivers that move the galvos. Signals and voltage from the
supply unit are sent to the CP- Light board and then routed to the X and Y galvos. CP- Light versions used in 7340/7440 lasers use
a larger capacity EEPROM than previous versions of CP- Light boards. The larger capacity is necessary to store multiple sets of tun -
ings for the marking head. There are different tuning sets each for the three and ten meter umbilicals, as well quality and speed
tuning sets.
There are two different generations of CP- Light that could be fitted in 7340/7440 lasers or in spare parts. It is important to note
which generation is present, as a minimum version of CMark is required for each. CP- Light 4 boards require a minimum CMark
4.7.6 and are blue in color. CP- Light 5 boards in 7340/7440 require minimum CMark 4.15.2 and are red in color. If CMark version
loaded on the laser is lower than required, the system will assert Marking Head errors.
F IGURE 3- 51: CP- Light 4 with mounting plate.
137
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 3- 52: CP- Light 5 without mounting plate.
F IGURE 3- 53: Example of errors received when CMark version is not new enough to support CP- Light 5.
Always a Set
The CP- Light board is always paired with a marking unit and these should not ever be separated. A USB drive
with a Lens Compensation file will accompany any spare marking unit or CP- Light. This file should be loaded to
the laser via the Web Interface (HiRes) or TCS+ (File Upload) and the USB drive stored in a safe location as a
backup. These files must be reloaded in the event of a Chameleon failure. After installing the Lens Com -
pensation files, also create a CMark (Firmware) backup as an additional backup of these critical files.
138
Rev AC

Supply Unit
Code Written to CP- Light at Boot
All CP- Light boards have a code written to them at the first boot after installation. This code is specific to the
type of laser include wattage or umbilical length (depending on which laser series). Once the code is written to
the CP- Light, that board and its associated galvo set cannot be used in a laser of a different wattage/model
number. For example, a CP- Light first used in a 7340 (3 meter umbilical) cannot later be used in a 7440 (10
meter umbilical) even if the marking unit type is the same. Errors that occur when code on CP- Light does not
match laser configuration are Wrong LIC Detected E00212 or a wrong configuration error.
TCS+ in CMark 4.7 or newer allows for reset of the CP- Light code so that a CP- Light/galvo set can be used in a
different laser if the configuration is not identical. After the code is reset through TCS+, a new code will be writ -
ten to the CP- Light on the next boot. For instructions on resetting CP- Light code see "Reset CP- Light Code" on
page 339 .
F IGURE 3- 54: CP- Light location identified - CP- Light 4 shown.
l
The CP- Light board receives signals from the Chameleon via the Head Interface Controller (HIC). This includes LVDS.
l
The 48V power supply in the supply unit provides power to the CP- Light board.
l
LVDS are sent from the HIC to the CP- Light board via a CAT7 cable .
l
The LVDS commands for moving the galvos are generated by the Chameleon and transmitted through the HIC.
l
The X and Y- mirrors are driven using Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) using drivers on the back of the board and sent via X-
and Y- mirror current connectors on the front of the board.
l
Shutter commands and feedback are also routed to/from the Chameleon and HIC to CP- Light.
139
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
l
There are two thermistors located in the marking head to send temperature data back to the CP- Light. Only one is currently
used (brown/white wires on X7 galvo connection) and is displayed in System Information screen in TCS+ as Marking Head
Temperature. A marking head temperature warning is generated when the temperature at the thermistor reaches 62°C and
an error asserts at 65°C. The other (red/purple wire connection at CP- Light) is reserved for future use. The temperature at
the CP- Light board is monitored and displayed in System Information in Marking Head section as Environmental Tem -
perature.
l
The LEC board with its red emission LEDs inside the marking unit is connected to the CP_ Light4 via the marking head cable.

Marking Unit
The marking unit is where the laser beam is focused and where the character formation is completed using X- and a Y- axis mir -
rors.
Available Marking Units
The marking unit is a single piece containing two lenses, two galvos, and several turning mirrors. The unit is paired with a CP_
Light board at manufacture and each pair is supplied with a USB drive containing the calibration files that must be loaded
through the Web Interface or TCS+ Service User. Spare marking units are supplied only as a single, complete spare part kit. Do
not separate the marking unit kit parts . Separating the individual kit parts to use individually will result in decreased mark
quality.
The calibration files included with the marking unit are based on a 1,000 point calibration routine performed with the specific
marking head in that kit. After installing the marking head, you will notice that print quality improves after the file from the USB
drive is uploaded to the laser.
143
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide

see "Marking Fields & Working Distances" on page 146 .

Identifying Marking Heads
The - S, - M, and - L versions of the Straight and Turned marking units cannot be differentiated by looking at the components. The
same size galvos are used in all marking units and the lenses are internal, so there is no physical way to differentiate. To identify
which head type you are looking at, locate the serial number on the marking head and look for the suffix at the end, which should
show - S, - M, or - L.
The marking unit type is also shown in the user interface.
l
In TCS+, this is shown at Laser Unit Settings in the Main Menu.
l
In SmartGraph, this is shown in Configuration and in the Marking Head tab.
l
In the Web Interface, this is shown in Laser Configuration in the Laser menu.
l
In the Clarity Laser Controller, this is shown in Tools | Diagnostics | Printhead | Scanner Type.

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Marking Fields & Working Distances
The marking fields, the largest possible area that is possible to be marked by the laser, is determined by the marking head unit
installed on the laser system. Each marking head unit has different lenses glued inside that create both the spot size and marking
field size .
F IGURE 4- 3: Orientation of A and B dimensions of marking fields.
Working Distance is the recommended distance from the marking head to the product. This is the point where the beam has the
most density (concentration of energy). The best distance for the marking unit to the product may occasionally vary in practice
based on the material being marked and the desired effect. The best working distance can vary across the marking field because
the lenses used in the 7340/7440 are not F- theta lenses. For more information on lenses and focal distance see "Importance of
Focal Distance" on page 153 .
F IGURE 4- 4: Measuring working distance on CFS (straight) and CFT (turned) marking unit.
Table 4- 1 on the facing page shows the Working Distances and Marking Fields for both the Straight marking unit (CFS) and the
Turned marking unit (CFT).

Turned Head Orientation
Note that the correct printing orientation of the CFT (turned) marking head will place the beam window at a slight angle. When
correctly oriented, the straight line on the end of the head will be vertically aligned as shown in Figure 4- 5 below . Therefore, the
window should not be parallel to the product as the mirrors produce a slightly angled output.

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Marking Unit Components
The marking unit includes all the components used to both create the correct size beam and also to create the code. Individual
major components of the marking unit are not replaceable individually. The small wear items such as O- rings are replaceable via
a kit. The marking unit as a spare part is paired with a CP- Light board and comes with a USB with a lens compensation file for
that marking head. This file must be uploaded onto the system as the last step in the installation. Never split up the marking unit
and its CP- Light board. Always install both pieces and load the compensation file or errors and poor print quality will occur. See
"Loading the Lens Compensation File" on page 430 for instructions on loading lens compensation file.
The main components in the marking unit are:
l
X- and Y- galvos
l
Beam expanding lens
l
Focusing lens
l
Turning mirrors
l
Focus finder mirror (circular)
148
Rev AC

Marking Unit
F IGURE 4- 6: Turned (top) and Straight (bottom) marking units.
F IGURE 4- 7: Interior of marking unit.
149
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
The beam path through the marking unit is quite complex. In addition the turning mirrors and lenses, the marking unit itself also
has a channeled path for airflow to circulate around critical components while remaining sealed from the optical components.
The beam path is as follows:
1. Expansion lens
2. First turning mirror
3. Focusing lens
4. X- galvo mirror
5. Second turning mirror
6. Third turning mirror
7. Y- galvo mirror
8. Output mirror (only on CFT marking heads)
F IGURE 4- 8: Turned marking unit beam path.
Lenses
Unlike many other lasers used for coding and marking, the marking unit of the 7340/7440 has the beam expanding and beam
focusing lenses before the galvos. These lenses are glued into the marking unit assembly and cannot be removed. If a different
size marking field is desired, the entire marking unit must be replaced.
Protective Glass
The glass that is visible from the outside of the marking head is not a lens, but rather protective glass. There are two layers of pro -
tective glass: One permanently mounted to the marking unit cover, and a removable protective window with frame that can be
removed for cleaning or replaced if damaged. These windows are only glass and do not change the beam size or characteristics,
though the glass should be cleaned regularly to avoid poor print quality or damage to internal components.
150
Rev AC

Marking Unit
F IGURE 4- 9: Location of screws that secure the outer protective glass window (s).
Galvos
An X- and Y- galvanometer (galvo) and mirror are fitted into the marking unit. These are aligned precisely and the marking unit
itself is paired with a CP- Light board and calibration file included on system or, if a spare, then the file is provided with marking
unit and CP- Light on a USB.
The galvos are used to deflect the beam in order to create the code to be marked and additionally function as the beam shutter
in the system. Due to the lower torque on the galvos as compared to other Videojet galvos, the amount of current required to
hold the galvo mirror in the shutter closed position is higher than other Videojet laser systems. The system has a 15 millisecond
shutter boost due to this need for higher current.
F IGURE 4- 10: Red arrow pointing to one of two galvos in marking unit.
151
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Temperature Sensors
There are two thermistors that are part of the marking head cable (umbilical) mounted in the marking unit. Only the sensor with
brown and white wires near the output end of marking unit is currently used to measure temperature in the marking unit with the
other reserved for future use.
F IGURE 4- 11: Red arrows indicating location of two thermistors mounted to marking unit.
Duckbill Valve
Air is directed around components in the marking unit for cooling, but is also used to create an air knife across the protective
glass on the outside of the marking unit. This air knife keeps the protective glass clean for a longer period of time and also cre -
ates a positive pressure so that no contaminants enter the marking unit through the marking head. The duckbill valve is a check -
valve that keeps contaminants from entering the air hose from the outside of the marking unit. This duckbill valve is replaceable
as part of the spares kit for O- rings. It is important that the duckbill is in the correct orientation when the marking unit is inserted
into the marking unit cover, as shown in Figure 4- 12 below .
F IGURE 4- 12: Correct orientation of duckbill valve in CFS (straight) and CFT (turned) marking units.
152
Rev AC

Marking Unit
Mounting for Collimator
The collimator, the part of the laser source that gives the beam its size, fits snugly into the marking unit and is held in place to the
marking unit by a small clip with two screws. It is very important to have this clip and the collimator positioned correctly into the
marking unit. Refer to Figure 4- 13 below .
F IGURE 4- 13: Location of collimator and collimator fixture when mounted in marking unit.
Importance of Focal Distance
The lenses used in the 7340/7440 are spherical and not F- theta lenses. This means that the Working Distance varies across the
marking field is slightly more than in some other Videojet laser systems. The following diagram shows the difference between an
F- theta lens and the spherical lenses used in the 7340/7440 laser system. In order to achieve the small size of the marking unit
the lenses are in place before the galvos and therefore F- theta lenses are not possible in this system. Only when the focusing lens
is past the galvos can F- theta lenses be used.
The following diagram shows the difference between an F- theta lens and the lenses used in the 7340/7440 laser system.
153
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 14: Comparison of working distance for F- theta and spherical lenses.
F IGURE 4- 15: Comparison of working distance on three types of Videojet marking heads.
Because the mark field is not flat, it may be necessary to raise or lower the marking head based on where within the marking field
your code will be located. The variation in actual working distance versus the nominal should not be more than a few millimeters.
The material being marked and the intended effect may also necessitate adjusting the marking head height to achieve the
desired effect. As you get further from the optimal focal point (up or down) your spot size will increase and the power density will
decrease.
154
Rev AC

Marking Unit
Focus Finder
The 7340/7440 laser system is equipped with a Focus Finder. When activated the marking head will project a red pilot beam dis -
playing a dot and a circle. As you move closer to the correct distance from the product for marking the dot and the circle will
begin to overlap. When the dot is inside the circle you are at the best focal point for that marking head.
F IGURE 4- 16: Focus Finder beam indicating out of focus (dot outside of circle).
155
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 17: Focus finder beam indicating in focus (dot inside circle).
The Focus Finder feature can be enabled through TCS+. Go to Line Setup | Edit Current Line Setup and click on the Focus Finder
tab. For additional information, see "Focus Finder Activation" on page 378 .
Pilot Laser
The pilot laser is a secondary laser that produces a visible red beam, but does not mark substrate. With the clearly visible red light
beam of the pilot laser, system set up can be greatly simplified. Controlled by software, the pilot continuously traces the job con -
tents to be marked. This provides the user the ability to check accuracy before any real mark and correct the settings if necessary.
The wavelength of the pilot laser beam is 630- 670 nanometers.
The pilot laser is generated by the same laser source that generates the laser beam so the pilot laser is standard on all
7340/7440 laser systems. The pilot laser is turned on and off through the user interface (TCS+, SmartGraph, or Clarity Controller ),
though TCS+ offers additional patterns and options that are not available through SmartGraph or Clarity Controller .
Use Proper Eye Protection
The Pilot laser is a Class II laser, but because the pilot laser is part of the beam source separation of Class II
and the infra- red Class IV beam cannot be guaranteed. Use either proper beam shielding or proper eye pro -
tection suited for the Class IV beam when activating the pilot laser.
Benefits of the pilot laser include:
l
Precise check of the data conversion on the laser system before the marking.
l
Facilitation of settings as for example the reference point setting.
l
User- friendly and maintenance- free.
l
Powered on/off via user interface.
156
Rev AC

Marking Unit
Laser Emission Control (LEC) Board
The Laser Emission Control (LEC) board contains LEDs which are lit when the laser system is armed. The LEC is part of the safety
system within the laser and the system will not fire if the LEC is not functional. If any of the LEDs on the board fail, then the
board must be replaced. The LEC also provides electrical shielding contact via metalized shielding holes.
The LEC and the red LED membrane keypad must both function or a fatal error (24V Red LED is Defective) will occur. The LEC
board is connected via the marking head cable to the CP- Light board.
Location
The LEC is mounted at the end of the umbilical between the sealed end of umbilical and the marking unit and red light is visible
through the plastic housing on outside of umbilical and marking unit junction.
F IGURE 4- 18: Arrow showing LEC board inside end of umbilical at marking unit.
157
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 19: Arrow indicating the LEC window.

Installation
The laser system accessories can be connected to the system both externally and internally. Externally, accessories connect via a
quick connector plate. Internally, accessories are connected via a cable passage plate or via the standard hybrid connector plate.
When using the hybrid connector plate, some accessories are connected at the Chameleon Customer Interface while others are
connected on external Quick Connectors.
Detailed descriptions of available inputs and outputs are found in the Customer Interface (Internal Connectors) section. The Cus -
tomer Interface (External) section contains information on how to connect, but not detailed descriptions on all inputs and out -
puts available in the system.
Installation Pre- Checks
Shock Hazard Warning
Use test equipment safely and properly.
1. Check the incoming voltage from the mains power receptacle with a multimeter.
2. Ensure placement of laser will allow for proper airflow and cable allowances as shown in the Operating Instructions.
3. Check the laser shipping carton for damage. Check the shock/tilt watches on packaging.
4. Unpack the laser and check the condition of laser system.
5. Verify that the laser system in the box is correctly configured as ordered (model, IP, wavelength, marking head, lens).
6. Check that all ordered accessories are included.
7. Connect the laser system to a temporary power supply.
8. Position a target at the correct focal distance from marking head.
9. Create a Test Pattern template in SmartGraph.
10. Take sample mark on the target using the Test Pattern template. Does the laser mark the product as expected?
If the laser does not create a mark with no peripheral devices (encoder, product detector, etc.), then do not proceed with
installation until the issue is resolved.
11. Mount the laser. The mounting hole pattern can be found in the Operating Instructions for each laser model.
12. Set the customer interface voltage source on X19 and X20. The default configuration is 24V Internal source. See "X19 &
X20 - Supply Voltage" on page 92 .
13. Connect the accessories. See "Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)" on page 193 for more on connecting when
using cable gland plate and "Customer Interface (External)" on page 219 when using quick connector plate.
14. Connect the safety system.
For 7340/7440 safety connection with double pole switch, see "SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring" on page 165 and "Safety System
Installation - CPD and FASS" on page 179 . If using a single pole switch see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on page 169
159
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
15. Configure the Product Registration/Line Setup. Use the Kompensation template for best results when using an encoder.
The Kompensation template will help to ensure good settings for Encoder Distance per Rotation, Angle of Laser, and Dir -
ection of Product Movement. See "Kompensation Template" on page 185 .
Use Customer's UI for Message Creation, Line Setup
Interfaces are not interchangeable. Do not mix and match during setup, as features are not identical
and mixing interfaces can cause issues.
l
If the customer will be using SmartGraph , perform setup using SmartGraph .
l
If the customer will be using TCS+, perform setup using TCS+.
l
If the customer is using Clarity , then perform setup with Clarity .
16. Adjust Parameter settings to get a good mark quality using the customer production line running at production speed.
17. Configure the IP address for Ethernet port (s), if necessary. You can set two IP addresses through the Web Interface (see
"Edit Laser Configuration" on page 397 ).

Marking Head Sensor Wiring
In order to avoid accidental beam output with the 7340/7440 model laser an optional Marking Head Sensor is available. The
sensor (PN: AL- SP80421) must be used together with the 3 or 10 meter cable which connects to the Customer Interface section
of Chameleon and one of the two marking head holders specific to the 7340/7440. Use of any other marking head holders will
cause the sensor not to function.
Marking Head Holders
Only the following marking head holders will function properly with the sensor. These holders have a special design with a metal
screw on the internal ring that attaches to marking head. The sensor only detects metal. Most ink jet marking head holders have
a plastic ring that surrounds the marking head and therefore the laser system will always display that the marking head is
removed since it isn't detecting the metal screw.
l
AL- SP79890 (EXTENSION KIT,HOLDER,DOVETAIL,CFX): Dovetail attachment - use to connect to flat dovetail- style brackets
l
AL- SP80425 (EXTENSION KIT,HOLDER,TRIPOD,TUBE,38MM,CFX): Tube attachment - use to connect to stands with 38 mm
tube

Installation
SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring
There are three ways to wire the SPM (3140/3340/7230/7330/7340/7440 models) or SPM- 16A (3640 model) to interlock or
shutterlock system. The method used is determined by the desired function. If laser model has an SPM- 16- A- FASS or a CPD3
board, please refer to the sections specific to those boards.
All procedures contained here use a double pole switch. If using a single pole switch, see "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on
page 169 .
The following options can be achieved with the SPM/SPM- 16A board:
l
Shutterlock (no Performance Level rating)
l
Interlock with or without manual reset switch (no Performance Level rating)
l
Emergency circuit with manual reset switch (compliant to Performance Level D)
Use Only Specified Parts
In order to avoid damage to components or to achieve stated Performance Level rating, it is important to use
the parts specified in the following wiring descriptions. Use of any parts other than those specified will result in
either no Performance Level rating or in damage to contacts of switches used for the interlock.
Shutterlock
For shutterlock functionality (mirrors go to shutter closed position, but laser is still powered), on connector X9 of the SPM/SPM-
16A remove the jumper on pins X9.5 to X9.16 and the jumper on X9.7 to X9.14. Connect double pole switch to pins 5 and 16
(shutterlock circuit 2) and pins 7 and 14 (shutterlock circuit 1). No reset switch is required for shutterlock function. Leave all other
jumpers in place.
When wired as a Shutterlock system, behavior is as follows when the door is opened:
1. Shutterlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to the shutter closed position
3. Laser tube remains powered and capable of emitting radiation
4. Red emissions LEDs remain on
5. Must press Start to restart the marking process.
165
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Interlock (No Performance Level)
To wire interlock with no Performance Level rating, remove jumpers 15- 19 and 17- 21 on connector X9 of SPM/SPM- 16A board.
Connect one circuit of double pole mechanical switch to pins 15 and 19 and other circuit to pins 17 and 21. Use of paired
screened cable is highly recommended. It is also recommended to create shield/ground connection on grounding bar (T shaped
cutouts in sheet metal between X9 and rear of supply unit cabinet). The grounding bar can also be used as strain relief for cables
by using cable ties to the T shapes.
Possible Equipment Damage - Mechanical Switches Only
The SPM/SPM- 16A Interlock circuit must be wired using mechanical switches or relays. Damage to contacts in
solid state/reed switches and relays will occur as the switches are directly driving relay coils on the SPM/SPM-
16A board. The peak current which is needed to switch the relays is too high for solid state/reed switches. The
Shutterlock circuit does not drive the relays on SPM/SPM- 16A, so solid state/reed switches may be used for
Shutterlock.
Lasers operated with 115 VAC should only use Shutterlock as peak current is too high for most mechanical
switches/relays. Lasers operated with 230 VAC can use Interlock, but should use mechanical switches/relays
Using a manual reset switch is optional when no Performance Level is needed. To install a manual reset switch:
1. Remove the jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 connector.
2. Connect a push button (mushroom button) style switch between pins 1- 23.
It is necessary to use a switch that pops back up on its own for the reset. If a toggle or other switch that stays closed
when depressed is used is in the closed position when laser is booted a fatal error will occur.
Behavior when wired for Interlock (with or without Performance Level):
1. Interlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to shutter closed position
3. Laser tube powered down
4. Red emissions LEDs go off
5. If using manual reset switch, reset button must be pushed and released or Interlock Open status continues
6. Must press Start to restart the marking process
166
Rev AC

Installation
Emergency Circuit with Manual Reset Switch
In order to achieve Performance Level D, the following instructions and specified parts must be used or no Performance Level rat -
ing will be reached. Deviation of any sort will result in no Performance Level rating.
l
You must use a Schmersal AZ- 16- 02ZVRK (minimum contact load 12 V / 5 mA) switch. You will also need to order an actu -
ator for this part based on the physical setup of safety enclosure. Videojet does not carry these parts.
l
Use paired, screened (shielded) cable.
l
Install a reset switch (push button or mushroom button; must pop back up after being depressed).
l
You must use 24V_ fused output on X9 for Reset Switch to maintain Level D rating and to avoid errors causing an Interlock
Open status even when closed.
Wiring Diagram
Figure 5- 6 on the next page shows wiring for both an interlock and a shutterlock. Typically only one or the other will be used.
Which is used depends on the desired behavior.
l
If wiring for an interlock , leave Shutterlock jumpers (X9.5 to X9.16 and X9.7 to X9.14) in place.
l
If wiring for a Shutterlock , leave Interlock jumpers (X9.15 to X9.19 and X9.17 to X9.21) in place.
l
Not shown in diagrams: X9.3 (power) and X9.13 (ground) are connected to the laser power supply. Laser power supply out -
put voltage is measured at SPM/SPM16A/SPM16A- FASS/CPD boards.
Figure 5- 6 on the next page shows a reset switch for the Interlock connected from pin 1 to pin 23. If no Performance Level is
required, leave the jumper from pin 1 to pin 23 in place. The system will not function correctly without either a jumper or a nor -
mally open reset switch connected between pins 1- 23.

F IGURE 5- 6: Diagram: Wiring for an interlock or shutterlock with reset switch shown.
Figure 5- 6 above suggests the wiring and parts needed to achieve a Performance Level D for Interlock (Emergency Circuit). You
must use paired, screened cables, a reset switch, and a specific switch (Schmersal AZ 16- 02ZVRK) or equivalent to achieve this Per -
formance Level rating.
Though Shutterlock is shown in this drawing, you cannot achieve a Performance Level Rating using only SPM/SPM- 16A. You must
use CPD+FASS for most Chameleon lasers or an SPM- 16A- FASS and a FASS board in 3640 lasers to achieve a Performance Level
Rating with Shutterlock.
It is recommended that incoming wires (if shielded) are stripped just inside the supply unit and tied down using cable ties to the
grounding bar near the rear of the supply unit. This creates a shield ground on these cables.

Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD
The safety system of Videojet lasers is designed to use a double pole switch. The use of a double pole switch builds in a redund -
ant circuit in case of failure of one side.
A single pole switch may be used to control the Shutterlock or Interlock functions, but because the system was designed to rely
on a redundant safety switching system, the wiring configurations detailed in the following sections must be used.
Damage to CPD
It is not sufficient to use a single pole switch on one of the Interlock or Shutterlock circuits and to leave the
default jumper for the other channel in place. On a CPD board, when only one channel is used (second channel
left jumpered) for Interlock, you will cause long- term damage to the relays on the CPD board.
Interlock/Shutterlock Open Won't Reset
On an SPM/SPM- 16A, when you only use one channel for Interlock and leave other channel jumpered the
Interlock Open will not reset unless you reboot the system. Use wiring detailed here for single pole switch to
avoid malfunction.
If a FASS board is present then a double pole switch should always be used and Interlock with FASS always requires a manual
reset switch to release the interlock. When FASS board is present and wiring as Interlock you must also supply a separate manual
reset switch as well.
169
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Interlock Single Pole Switch Wiring
If using a single pole switch, then replace one Interlock jumper and install the switch in its place. Then, install jumpers from each
leg of the switch to its second channel as shown in Figure 5- 8 on the facing page .
l
Note regarding CPD : If you only replace one jumper with a switch and leave the second jumper in place as is, the relay will
struggle to latch and a clicking sound will be heard while the Interlock is open. Leaving the wiring like this long- term will
cause damage to the board.
l
Note regarding SPM or SPM16- A : If you only remove one jumper and replace with single pole switch once interlock is
opened you will not be able to reset without rebooting.
Use the Figure 5- 8 on the facing page and Figure 5- 9 on page 172 to wire Interlock function on CPD/SPM/SPM- 16A using single
pole switch.

The safety connector on the Quick Connector plate is not recommended for use in systems with FASS boards

Trigger Enable
The active low input Trigger Enable and 24V output are also wired to the safety system rear panel quick connector. Trigger
Enable must be held high in order to acknowledge trigger inputs. This input is not always used by customer and with quick con -

Prevent shorting of 24V output
Remember to cap gray and pink wires coming from AL- 75007 if not connecting Trigger Enable and 24V on cus -
tomer side or shorting may occur.
Convert from Interlock to Shutterlock
The safety system quick connector is wired by default to behave as Interlock (no FASS present)/Emergency Circuit (FASS
present). This behavior includes turning off power to the laser tube and requires a 5 second delay after the Interlock/Emergency
Circuit is reset.
If the preferred behavior is for the tube to stay energized and when door is opened and mirrors to move to Shutter Closed pos -
ition, then it is necessary to reassign the wires inside the supply unit that connect the quick connector with the X 9 connector of
CPD3 or SPM board.
The following procedure explains how to change from Interlock to Shutterlock.
Personal Injury - Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Before
starting work, ensure that the laser system is completely detached from its power source. The Laser system is
energized as long as laser is plugged into power outlet.
1. Remove jumpers (two) on pins 7- 14 and pins 5- 16 on X9 connector of CPD3 or SPM board.
2. Move the following wires on X9 of CPD3 or SPM board coming from the safety system quick connector on rear panel of
supply unit based on Table 5- 3 below .
Table 5- 3: Wires to move, X9
Wire Color Signal Move from Move to
Green Channel 1 out X9.21 X9.7
Brown Channel 1 return (in) X9.17 X9.14
White Channel 2 out X9.19 X9.5
Yellow Channel 2 return (in) X9.15 X9.16
3. Insert one of the jumpers removed in step 1 into X9.15 - X9.19 and the other jumper into X9.17 - X9.21.
4. Connect the cable on the customer side to the door switch using the wire colors shown in the rightmost column of Table 5-
4 on the facing page .

Single Pole Switch with Quick Connect
The quick connector for the safety system is intended for use with a double pole door switch. If a single pole switch will be used
then it is necessary to reassign wires inside supply cabinet on X9 connector of CPD/SPM board using the following instructions.
Interlock with Single Pole and Quick Connect
1. Disconnect White and Yellow wires from X9 on CPD/SPM. Cut or cap these wires to prevent shorting.
2. Place jumper from pin 15 to pin 17 on X9. There will now be both the jumper and Brown wire on pin 17 .
3. Place jumper from pin 19 to pin 21 on X9. There will now be both the jumper and Green wire on pin 21 .
4. Connect customer single pole switch to Green (out) and Brown (in) wires of AL- 75007 cable.
5. Cut or cap White and Yellow wires on AL- 75007 cable.
177
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
6. If Trigger Enable will be used, connect Trigger Enable to the customer side using the Pink and Gray wires.
- OR-
If Trigger Enable will not be used:
6.1. Remove the Pink and Gray wires at X25 pins 9 and 12.
6.2. Place a jumper between X25 pins 9 and 12.
6.3. Cut or cap the Pink and Gray wires on both the inside of the supply unit and on the cable.
Shutterlock with Single Pole and Quick Connect
1. Remove and cut or cap White and Yellow wires on both the inside and outside of the quick connector as well as the cable to
customer.
2. Remove two jumpers on X9: remove the jumper between pin 5 and pin 16 and remove the jumper between pin 7 and pin
14. Retain these jumpers for use in following steps.
3. Inside the supply unit, on X9 move the Green wire coming from safety connector over from X9 pin 21 to X9 pin 7.
4. Move the Brown wire from X9 pin 17 to X9 pin 14.
5. Place a jumper from X9 pin 7 to X9 pin 5. You should now have a jumper and Green wire in pin 7.
6. Place a jumper from X9 pin 14 to X9 pin 16. You should now have both a jumper and Brown wire in pin 14.
7. Place a jumper on X9 between pins 15 and 19.
8. Place a jumper on X9 between pins 17 and 21.
9. Connect the Brown (in) and Green (out) wires on the AL- 75007 cable to the customer single pole door switch.
10. Connect Trigger Enable and 24 V via AL- 75007 at the customer side.
- OR-
Remove the pink and gray wires from the X25 connector on the Customer Interface of the Chameleon board and
instead place a jumper between pins 9 and 12 on X25.
See "Trigger Enable" on page 175 for more information.

Safety System Installation - CPD and
FASS
The following descriptions pertain to installation of Shutterlock, Interlock, and/or Emergency Switch on units with FASS board
installed in addition to the CPD.
The following is the expected behavior with CPD+FASS:
l
Shutterlock - Shutter closes, but laser power remains on. No reset from Emergency Stop Reset required.
l
Interlock - Shutter closes and laser is powered off. Requires reset from Emergency Stop Reset (S02)
Safety System Selection
Always select the correct safety system configuration in the laser Web Interface.
For instructions on viewing or editing the safety system settings see "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 397 .
179
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Connecting to CPD X9 (Cable Glands)
Connection to the safety system is accomplished through the CPD board X9 connector. Two variations are shown in Figure 5- 12
on the facing page .
l
Shutterlock (1): Connect door switch 1 to pins 7 and 14 and door switch 2 to pins 5 and 16. Leave all other jumpers in place.
l
Interlock (2): Emergency switch 1 to pins 17 and 21 and Emergency switch 2 to pins 15 and 19 AND Emergency Switch
Reset to pins 1 - 23. Leave all other jumpers in place. Emergency Switch Reset should always be a push button switch that is
open except to reset after doors are opened and closed.

jumpers in place.

Installation
Kompensation Template
The purpose of the Kompensation Test is to fine tune the Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between the Laser and Direction of
Product Movement in the Product Registration. The test is highly recommended for applications involving a 2D data matrix bar
code, but should be used in most installations to achieve most accurate results on setup of encoder.
The following procedure is described using the SmartGraph software.
Importing the Kompensation Template
1. Download the Kompensation.tpl template to a location on your computer.
You can find the Kompensation template file in the VTI Support & Technology team space.
2. Launch SmartGraph .
3. Connect to the Laser in the File tab. Ensure that after connection, the Laser side (right hand side) of the File tab is high -
lighted. The highlighted side is where the imported file will be saved.
4. Click Import .
5. Select the Import File option from the drop- down menu.
6. Browse to the Kompensation template on your computer. Ensure that the Type of File in the bottom of the window is
set to look for Templates (*.tpl).
185
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
7. Select the template and click Open .
8. Verify that the Kompensation template now appears in the list of Templates on the right hand side of screen in the File
tab.
Setting the Product Registration
1. Ensure that the Laser side of File tab is active, indicated by an orange banner above the files section.
2. Click the Configuration tab.
3. Click the Marking Head tab.
4. Set the Marking Head orientation based on the orientation of the marking head relative to the product. A small picture
will show the orientation of the head based on your choice. Adjust until the orientation matches the mounting of the laser.
5. Click Apply .
186
Rev AC

Installation
6. Click the Product Registration tab .
7. Click New to create a new product registration.
8. Name your file and click OK.
9. Make the necessary adjustment to your product registration base on Trigger type, Start Distance, and Blocking.
10. Enter twice the pulses per rotation of your encoder. For example, if a 2000 pulse per rotation encoder enter 4000 pulses in
the Product Registration.
11. Enter the diameter of encoder wheel (if present) in the Pulses Per Rotation box.
12. Enter the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement . Use the chart below to assist in assessing the
setting for both Mounting Position and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement (abbreviated Product
Movement Angle).
187
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Setting the Parameters
The Kompensation template imports with a Parameter set called AP08111318.
Product Movement Too Fast Faults
Depending on the conveyor line speed you may have to adjust the parameters to avoid Product Movement
Too Fast faults.
188
Rev AC

Installation
Adjust the Marking Speed to a speed fast enough to avoid Product Movement Too Fast errors, but slow enough to create a mark
on the product.
Printing the Kompensation Template
1. Click on the Marking tab.
2. Click on the Marking Adjustment tab.
3. Select the Kompensation template from the drop- down list.
4. Click on the Marking tab.
189
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
5. Press the Start button.
6. The laser status should indicate Waiting for Trigger. If this is not displayed, check that you have permissions to fire the
laser and that the Trigger Enable input has 24 volts applied (often jumpered X25.9- X25.12).
See "Permissions" on page 404 .
7. As the product passes through the product detector you should see the Marking indication. If not, adjust the Start
Distance in your Product Registration.
Troubleshooting
To center the product in the print area, adjust the Start Delay in Product Registration.
If message is unreadable, check the Angle of Product Movement in Product Registration.
If message is of correct orientation but of poor quality, adjust the Parameters.
Understanding the Results
If both the Distance Per Rotation in encoder setting and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are correct
then the mark from the Kompensation template should be a cross with no curves on either axis as shown in Figure 5- 14 below .
F IGURE 5- 14: A mark indicating correct Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are
both correct.
Make adjustments to correct for curvature in the horizontal or vertical axis.
190
Rev AC

Installation
Setting the Correct Mounting Position
Once the correct Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement is found, it may be necessary to
adjust the Mounting Position in the Marking Head tab. If conveyor line direction is left to right, then the Mount -
ing Position should be adjusted to the same number as the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product
Movement. If conveyor direction is right to left, then Mounting position should be adjusted to 180 degree off
from Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement.

Customer Interface
(Internal Connectors)
Inputs and outputs for the 7340/7440 laser system are connected via the Customer Interface (CI) located on the Chameleon
board. Depending on the rear panel installed on the laser, these connections to CI may be done via Quick Connectors or Cable
Glands. Quick Connectors are pre- wired from the rear panel to the CI. Pass- through cable glands allow cables to run through for
making direct connections to the CI. In both cases, the CI is the same and it is only the method used to connect that varies.
The CI is comprised of seven connectors.
Table 6- 1: Customer Interface Connecter Descriptions
Connector Description
X21 Suction Control
X22 Variable I/O
X25 Line Interface
X27 Variable I/O
X29 Variable I/O
X30 Variable I/O
X33 Internal Connection
193
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
CI Connectors Location
The Customer Interface connectors are located along the edge of the Chameleon board near the serial and Ethernet ports.
194
Rev AC

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Default Bridges
There are several inputs on the CI that are active lows and when the input is 0V or not connected, an error condition will occur.
Error conditions are avoided by placement of default bridges (jumpers) to hold active low outputs in a high state by connecting
to 12V/24V outputs. These default bridges may be removed if external devices will be connected to the active low inputs or may
be left in place after installation if the active low inputs will not be utilized.

X21.3- 5

Voltage Supply for CI
The Customer Interface section must be supplied with power and ground either through internal supply from the Chameleon
board or from supply by the customer using an external source. Setup for supplying the CI with power and ground is done by
moving jumpers on X19 and X20 on the Chameleon board and by connection of external power and ground (external only).
More information on configuring and connecting the supply for CI can be found in "X19 & X20 - Supply Voltage" on page 92 .

X21 on the Customer Interface of the Chameleon board is dedicated to fume extractor signals. Both the remote start function
for a fume extractor and monitoring of faults from a connected fume extractor can be accomplished through connection to X21.

Input Descriptions
l
Error Status Customer - Allows for input of error from peripheral device. This input was known as External Error on ACC3-
based systems. Input is an Active Low, so if it is not used it must be tied to 12V/24V. This is bridged by default from man -
ufacturing to pin 7 to tie signal high.
l
Error Confirm - 12V/24V input confirms, but does not delete or save errors that have occurred on the laser system. Using
Error Confirm remotely completes same action as pressing Confirm button in SmartGraph or other user interface.
Output Description
l
Error - The system is stopped immediately if an error occurs during the marking process. The output is reset with the next
Start, but may be set again if the error was not corrected. Active low signal.
l
System Ready - Output when system has booted with no errors and is ready to receive inputs. Any error will result in Sys -
tem Ready signal to be set to low. Signal is an active high.
l
Ack_ Job_ Selection - A high level indicates that the job selection process is finished. Low level indicates that the job selec -
tion process was not completed. Typically used with External Template Selection. Signal is a high active.

Table 6- 8: Chameleon Board Inputs
Name Description Polarity
Marking Head Removed from Stops the marking process and creates a Fault if connected to Low Active
Holder optional sensor.
l
The sensor is high when the metal portion of the marking
head holder is present .
l
The sensor is low when the metal portion of marking

l

This is an additional fault only for 7340/7440.
Start Marking Starts the marking process, waits for a trigger signal, reacts to High active
rising edge. Start has no function if Stop Marking is set to low.
Stop Marking Stops the marking process. This signal has to be set to high if Low active
not used (bridge to 24V). If Stop is low then Start Marking has
no function.
The Start and Stop inputs can control the marking process with signals from a PLC or other external device. The Stop Marking out -
put, if not used, must be tied to 12V/24V as this is an active low input. If 12V/24V is not applied to Stop Marking then the con -
dition is always active and therefore constantly in Stop mode with Start disabled.
The Marking Head Removed from Holder input is an active low input that works with marking head holders that have a metal
internal marking head holder. When connected, the sensor creates a fault when the marking head is removed from the outer por -
tion of holder. This sensor only senses metal and does not work with traditional ink jet marking head holders in which both pieces
are plastic.

by the laser. The system is ready to mark and waits for a trigger signal.

Default Bridges
Several default bridges are put in place during manufacturing to hold active low inputs in the high state to avoid errors. These
are:
l
Pin 3 - 7: Marking Head Removed from Holder to Stop Marking (since Stop Marking will have 12V/24V applied Marking
Head Removed from Holder will share the 12V/24V)
l
Pin 7 - 9: Stop Marking to Input None (will have 12V/24V applied by another bridge)
l
Pin 9 - 11: Input None to 24V
If the Stop Marking input will be used, remove the bridge to pin 7 and connect 12V24V from customer equipment.

X33 - Internal Connections
Most connections on X33 on the Customer Interface (CI) should be left with default bridges in place. The bridges in place on the
X33 connector allow for functionality of the safety system including CPD and FASS boards.
Location
F IGURE 6- 3: X33 location.
Description
Table 6- 10: X33 Pin Descriptions
Pin Direction Description
1 IN External Key Switch In
8 OUT External Key Switch Out
The only function that can be used on X33 is the Remote Key switch. By connecting remote switch to pins 1 and 8, the user could
activate laser power remotely. If this functionality is desired then either 1) the key switch inside supply cabinet must be bridged
or 2) the physical key left in the ON (armed) position all the time.
Default Bridges
Do not remove default bridges (jumpers) with the exception of External Key Switch. They are responsible for functionality of CPD
and FASS (where installed).

Connecting an Encoder & Product Detector
The 7340/7440 supports only quadrature encoders. When configuring the Encoder in SmartGraph software the pulses per rota -
tion must be doubled in Product Registration/Line Setup. Do not double the pulses for the encoder in TCS or Clarity Laser Con -
troller.
Product detect signals must be PNP (sourcing). Pin 4 provides supply voltage for product detect and ground is provided on pin 8.
Encoder & Trigger Detection
F IGURE 6- 5: Encoder and product detect connection.
206
Rev AC

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Figure 6- 5 on the previous page shows the typical connection for an encoder and product detector for the 7340/7440 . It is not
necessary to use the Encoder Index track unless needed in the specific application. The Encoder Index track may be used as a
Trigger in some applications, but in that case the Encoder Index track wire should be connected to pin 7 (Trigger) rather than pin
5 (Encoder Index). It is also necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 so that the system senses a load between these pins.
The laser system inputs are able to identify automatically if an encoder or product detect are connected.
l
If a product registration/line setup is selected that uses product movement with an encoder, the software checks to see if
there is a load between X25.2 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.6). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No Encoder
Attached). The same mechanism is implemented for the trigger.
l
If a product registration/line setup is selected which uses a product detector (Rising/Falling Edge or External), the software
checks if there is a load between X25.4 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.8). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No
Product Detector Attached).
The minimum load to be identified by the system for encoder or product detect is 20 mA. If no load is connected, either a No
Encoder Connected  or No Product Detector Connected message will assert.
Trigger Detection with no Trigger Attached Errors
No Trigger or Encoder Attached Errors
If using a dry contact as a Trigger, such as a fire signal from a PLC, or sharing encoder between two lasers,
then it is necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 (trigger) or 2- 6 (encoder) on X25 so that resistance/load is
sensed by the laser system. This is only necessary in CMark versions older than CMark version 3.4. In lasers with
CMark version 3.4 or newer it is possible to disable these errors in the laser's Web Interface and no resistor will
be needed.
See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 397 .
207
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 6- 6: A resistor employed to allow use of a dry contact as a trigger.
Use of a Dry Contact as a Trigger with no Trigger Detection (CMark 3.4 or newer)
Beginning with CMark version 3.4, you may disable the errors associated with no load on the Trigger supply pin by logging into
the Web Interface and toggling ON the box next to Disable Trigger | Encoder Detached Errors . After changing this setting,
reboot the laser.
No resistor will be needed, but it will be necessary to use pin X25.12 (24V supply interface) as the Supply pin for the Trigger
(24V).
F IGURE 6- 7: A dry contact as trigger with no resistor.
208
Rev AC

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
X30 - External Template Selection
Signals for External Template Selection are received via X30. External Template Selection allows a remote device such as a PLC to
remotely change the current template. The external device sends a 24V binary pulse signal representing a number. That number
matches a template in an already compiled template list in SmartGraph. After receiving the 24V signal on the Strobe input, the
laser system changes to the template matching the number received.
Location
209
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Description
Table 6- 12: X30 Pin Descriptions
Pin Direction Description
X30.1 IN JobSelect0
X30.2 IN JobSelect1
X30.3 IN JobSelect2
X30.4 IN JobSelect3
X30.5 IN JobSelect4
X30.6 IN JobSelect5
X30.7 IN JobSelect6
X30.8 IN JobSelect7
X30.9 IN JobSelectStrobe
X30.10 GND_ CI
X30.11 +24V_ CI
X30.12 GND_ CI
Up to 8 wires coming from the customer's PLC or other device are landed on the X30 connector representingup to 8 bits. The 24V
signal that actually executes the template change is connected on pin 9. All incoming signals must be held for at least 10 mil -
liseconds in order to be recognized.
F IGURE 6- 8: Template selection wiring.
For more information on External Template Selection contact Technical Support.

A typical use case for signals from X27 would be to monitor Good shot and Bad shot. Following are descriptions of inputs and out -
puts on X27.
l
Shutdown - 12V/24V pulse to this input will initiate shutdown of laser system. Same function as pressing Standby button
on outside of supply unit.
l
PC Connected - Output is set as soon as a PC with client application is connected. Signal is High Active.
l
Good - Indicates that the last marking has been completed without a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next
trigger signal. Signal is Low Active.
l
Bad - Indicates that the last marking was not completed due to a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next trig -
ger signal. Signal is Low Active.
Pin 7 is bridged to X33.3 in order to enable the CPD and FASS safety systems. Do not remove this bridge.

No Trigger Detector Connected Error
If a product registration/line setup which uses a product detector (Rising/Falling Edge or External) the software
checks if there is a load between X25.4 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.8). If no load is detected an error will be gen -
erated (E10814: No Trigger Detector Attached).
If using a dry contact as a Trigger, such as a fire signal from a PLC, then it is necessary to use a resistor across
pins 4- 8 (trigger) so that resistance/load is sensed by the laser system. This is only necessary in CMark versions
older than CMark version 3.4.
In lasers with CMark version 3.4 or newer it is possible to disable these errors in the laser's Web Interface and
no resistor will be needed.
See "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 397 .

quick connector plate or the 4 way DIN connector and cable may be used in conjunction with other quadrature encoders.

Encoder Detached Error
If not using 24V to supply encoder, such as in situations where one encoder is shared by multiple lasers, it is
necessary to either use a 4.7 KOhm resistor from X25.2- 6 in order to create a load on X25.2 (24V supply for
encoder) or error No Encoder Attached (E10813) will occur in lasers with CMark prior to version 3.4. In lasers
with CMark 3.4 or newer, it is not necessary to use a resistor, but you must log in to Laser Web Interface and
toggle the Disable Trigger/Encoder Detached Errors option.
Beacon Alert Light
The with a quick connector panel is able to use several beacons based on desired cord length.
l

l
CIJ beacon kit (PN: SP399089) with 15 inch cable
Wiring Laser Alert Light (Beacon)
There are two styles of laser beacons; an older style with 5 wires including power and ground and new style with 4 wires. The
older style beacon should be wired as follows:
Table 7- 5: Alert Light (PN 376178) Version with 5 Wires
Beacon Wire Color Connect to Chameleon Signal
Red X29.2 Error
Amber X22.8 Shutter Closed
Green X22.6 Ready to Mark
Black X22.10 Ground
White X22.11 24V
224
Rev AC

Customer Interface (External)
Table 7- 6: Alert Light (PN 376178) Version with 4 Wires
Beacon Wire Color Connect to Chameleon Signal
Red X29.2 Error
Amber X22.6 Ready to Mark
Green X22.8 Shutter Closed
Common X22.11 24V
The new version of the laser alert light with 4 wires can only use input signals of the same polarity whereas the 5 wire version had
been modified to work with both high and low active signals. The 4 wire version uses the off (not) state to drive the amber and
green lights on the alert light.
Using the wiring shown in Table 7- 6 above , the green light will illuminate when the Shutter Closed output is not active and there -
fore the shutter is open which occurs while in the marking mode. The amber light is illuminated when the laser is not Ready to
Mark. This state occurs when the Stop signal has been received and no errors are present. Both the red and amber lights will be lit
when an error occurs due to the low states of both the Error and Ready to Mark signals.

With one color lit:
l
55 mA max for Green
l
45 mA max for Amber
l
50 mA max for Red
Xtract Pro, Max, & PVC Wiring
The Xtract Pro, Xtract PVC, and Xtract Max all use the same I/O cable for connection to the laser. Connection of the I/O cable to
the laser offers Remote Start functionality for suction on the fume extractor and/or filter full and fault outputs from the extractor
to the laser.
IO Cable Not Standard with Fume extractor Unit
Xtract Pro, Max, and PVC do not come with an I/O cable. Part number AL- 79461 (I/O cable extraction unit, 7
meter) must be ordered if the fume extractor is to be connected to a laser for remote start of suction or error
monitoring.

AD- Access/AD- Oracle/AD- PVC extractors.

Quick Connector Procedure (DIN)
1. Disassemble the DIN connector:
1.1. Insert the DIN connector into the male 5 pin DIN connector located on the rear of the laser unit. This will provide
leverage to disassemble the DIN.
1.2. Use an adjustable wrench to unscrew the black plug at end of DIN connector.
F IGURE 7- 10: Adjustable wrench used to remove black plug
1.3. Once the plug is removed, place the wrench on the two flat sides of the DIN and loosen the connector.
1.4. Disassemble the connector.
229
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
2. Pull the cable through the DIN Connector.
3. Strip back the outer cable to expose the Shielding.
4. Separate the individual wires and strip approximately two millimeters to expose the wire ends.
5. Roll the shielding back onto the cable. This will allow for contact with the DIN connector or shape the shielding for
installing in the Chameleon Customer Interface X21 connector.
6. Using the 0.9 millimeter hex key, loosen the screws slightly to prepare for installation of each wire.
7. Insert the wires according to Table 7- 7 on page 228 and tighten the set screws.
230
Rev AC

Customer Interface (External)
8. Once the shielding is in place and the wires are securely connected, begin to reassemble the DIN connector.
9. Set the rocker switch position on interior, upper section of fume extractor to Start/Stop Function On.
Connect to Chameleon Procedure
1. Strip back the outer cable to expose the Shielding.
2. Separate the individual wires and strip approximately two millimeters to expose the wire ends.
3. Shape the shielding for installing in the Chameleon Customer Interface X21 connector.
4. Insert the wires according to Table 7- 8 on page 229 .
231
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
5. Set the rocker switch position on interior, upper section of fume extractor to Start/Stop Function On.
Xtract Lite Wiring
The Xtract Lite has an integrated I/O cable which can be used to remotely start the suction on the extractor or to generate a fault
on laser when the extractor is not working correctly.
Xtract Lite I/O Cable Wire Colors Differ from Other Extractors
The Xtract Lite has an I/O cable as a standard part. The Xtract Pro, Xtract Max, and Xtract PVC extractors have
an I/O cable as an option and the cable must be ordered separately for those units. These I/O cables do not
have the same wire colors and therefore connection will differ.
232
Rev AC

F IGURE 7- 11: Xtract Lite I/O cable
Differences from Legacy AD- Access Connection
On the earlier models, an extraneous blue wire included in the cable may be exposed. In more recent models, the blue wire is cut
flush with the cable in manufacturing. If the blue wire is visible, simply cut it flush to avoid confusion.

Fume Extractor Quick Connector - AD
Oracle and AD- Access
An external jumper plug accompanies all laser systems that are equipped with the quick connector panel. This jumper plug can
also be used to connect a fume extractor to the laser system. Connection allows the fume extractor to be remotely started when
the laser is in the Ready to Mark or other desired state so that it is not necessary to start and stop the fume extractor manually.
You can also order the fume extractor jumper plug (PN: AL- 76119) as a separate spare part for use during service/maintenance.
Connection requires use of 0.9 mm Allen wrench.
l
When using the DIN connector to connect a Fume extractor, a 0.9 mm Allen wrench is required for the lugs.
l
You can also use the fume extractor jumper plug (PN: AL- 76119) as a separate spare part for use during ser -
vice/maintenance.

Maintenance
Regular preventative maintenance is required in order to avoid unplanned downtime or the need to replace potentially expensive
components. This section details the recommended preventative maintenance for the laser system.
Maintenance Schedule
Perform the recommended preventative maintenance on the laser system at the intervals described in Table 8- 1 below . Fre -
quency may vary greatly based on ambient conditions. In extremely dirty ambient conditions, increase the frequency of fan filter
checks and marking head glass inspections.
Table 8- 1: Preventative Maintenance Schedule
Maintenance Interval Measures
l
Monthly (more frequently when Check the marking head protective glass for dust and dirt. Clean
operated in dirty ambient protective glass if dirt/debris are present.
conditions)
l
Check the fan filters on the front of the supply unit. Blow out or replace as
necessary.
l
When control lamp on Fume Replace filters on the fume extractor. The Combined Filter will usually
Extractor lights up require replacement more often than HEPA filter.
l
If the control lamp on the fume extractor remains lit after exchanging the
Combined Filter, change the HEPA filter.
l
Every six months (more frequently Exchange input (bulb style) and output (in- line) filters on compressor.
when operated in dirty ambient
l
Carry out a visual inspection of the laser system including warning labels.
conditions)
Warning labels must be readable and correctly positioned.
l
Check the product detector (light barrier). Clean or readjust as
necessary.
l
Check the fume extractor for air leaks.
l
Annually Exchange the HEPA filter (charcoal filter) on the fume extractor (if it has
not already been changed). As charcoal absorbs moisture from the air, it
becomes less effective.

Rev AC

Maintenance
Protective Glass Cleaning Procedure
There are two layers of protective glass on the outside of the marking unit. It will occasional be necessary to clean these layers of
protective glass as debris from the marking process build up on them. The outer layer (protective window) will need to be cleaned
more often than the layer which is glued to the marking unit housing. Both of these optical pieces are protective glass and are
not lenses.
F IGURE 8- 1: The protective glass on CFS marking unit.
F IGURE 8- 2: Arrow pointing to protective glass on CFT marking unit.
243
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
The lenses used to first expand and then to focus the beam are inside the marking unit housing and do not have any pre -
ventative maintenance requirement. The lenses and mirrors inside the head are sealed within the housing and should not get
dirty under normal operating conditions. One of the lenses is not accessible even when marking unit is opened.
The marking unit housing should never need to be removed for any preventative maintenance.
Cleaning the Protective Glass
Personal Injury - Shock Hazard Warning
Before starting any work the laser system must be dead!
Personal InjuryPersonal Injury
To prevent personal injury when handling these substances, always wear protective clothing and rubber gloves.
1. Turn the key switch to position 0 .
2. Remove the key to ensure that the laser system cannot be switched on.
3. Switch off the main switch.
4. Disconnect the mains connector.
5. Visually inspect the marking unit protective glass.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not touch the protective glass surface!
Avoid damage to the protective glass
l
Never use contaminated optics cleaning paper.
l
Make sure that the optics cleaning paper is stored in a clean place.
6. Fold a sheet of optics cleaning paper at least 5 times to create a flat cushion.
244
Rev AC

Maintenance
7. Hold the optics cleaning paper between thumb and index finger and put a few drops of acetone onto the optics clean -
ing paper. Methanol or Isopropyl alcohol (90% or higher preferred) is also acceptable.
8. Carefully and slowly pull the optics cleaning paper across the surface to be cleaned working from center to outer edge.
l
Make sure that you do no touch the surface with gloves!
l
Apply only very light pressure onto the paper!
245
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
9. If necessary, repeat the procedure with a new sheet of optics cleaning paper.
10. Inspect the protective glass. If it still looks dirty, it may be necessary to remove the exterior protective glass and frame
and to clean the inside of glass and also the protective glass that is glued into the marking unit housing.
Removing the Protective Glass Frame
If it is necessary to clean the inside of protective glass frame and marking unit window proceed as follows:
1. Turn the key switch to position 0 .
2. Remove the key to ensure that the laser system cannot be switched on.
3. Switch off the main switch.
4. Disconnect the mains connector.
5. Use a 1.5 mm Allen wrench to remove screw (s) holding the protective glass in place. Do not lose the o- ring inside the
protective frame .
246
Rev AC

Maintenance
6. Using a new wipe with solvent, clean the outside of window on the marking unit housing. Do not open the marking
unit, the internal components should not be cleaned.
7. Set the marking unit aside in a safe location where it will not fall.
8. Use the cleaning steps described in "Cleaning the Protective Glass" on page 244 to clean both sides of the glass that
make up protective frame. Set the frame down on a clean surface until you reinstall.
9. Reinstall the protective frame. Ensure the o- ring is still attached to the protective frame.
10. Tighten the screw (s) on the frame of the protective window with a 1.5 mm Allen wrench. Do not over- tighten .
247
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Complete the First Steps section first when troubleshooting marking issues or performance problems with a laser system.
First Steps
1. Clean the protective glass on the marking unit.
2. Ensure that the Working Distance is correct for head/lens combination (marking head to product distance).
3. Check that the product detector and encoder are connected and in serviceable condition.
4. Ensure the umbilical and other cables are in serviceable condition.
5. Power the laser ON.
l
Look up any Error messages
l
If the system does not power on, see "Troubleshooting - No Power" on page 317 .
6. Check the Product Registration/Line Setup.
l
Verify that the desired Product Registration is assigned to Template/Job
l
Verify that the settings in the Product Registration/Line Setup are correct for the application
l
Verify the Configuration settings (mark head type, lens type) are correct for the installed components
7. Fire in static mode to decide if the laser is capable of marking when no peripherals are involved.
To static fire:
l
If using SmartGraph, set Product Registration to No Product Movement and set Triggers to Auto and None
l
If using TCS or Clarity, set Line Setup to Static Product Movement and Internal Once Trigger

Error & Warning Categories
The type of Warning or Error detected by the laser system determines the behavior of the laser. Following are the types of warn -
ings and errors and the behavior of the laser system when detected.
Table 9- 2: Error & Warning Types with Category Symbols
Type Category
Fatal Error 0 Requires system to be rebooted
System Error 1 Power supply to laser turned off
Error 2 Marking is stopped
Warning W System continues to mark
Warnings
l
Marking may be continued with Warning conditions.
250

Troubleshooting
Errors
l
Yellow LED  on supply unit will flash.
l
System Errors (1) and Errors (2) must be confirmed in user interface before marking can be restarted.
l
Fatal Errors (0) cannot be confirmed and system must be restarted before marking can be restarted.
l
With all error classes the condition causing the error must be corrected or error will reoccur.
