Troubleshooting
Troubleshooting - No Power
Symptom: No light on Power/Standby button on the membrane keyboard.
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply.
1. Ensure the Laser system is plugged into mains power.
2. Check whether the green 48V Present LED  on the Chameleon board is illuminated.
3. Continue troubleshooting based on the state of the LED.
48V Present LED Is Not Illuminated
1. If the 48V Present LED  is not illuminated, measure voltage at the Chameleon board to verify 48V at X28.11 (48V) and
X.28.10 (Ground).
Recently Removed Mounting Plate
If work was recently completed that involved removing the mounting plate inside the supply unit prior
to loss of power, verify wiring at X28.
2. If no 48V, disconnect the system from mains power and check the two fuses in the rear of the supply unit.
3. If no 48V, check the mains power cord and try an alternative power cord.
4. If no 48V, check the output of the 48V power supply at the power supply. Possible defective parts include the 48V power
supply, the SPM or CPD3, or the internal harness.
48V Present LED Is Illuminated
If the 48V LED Present LED is illuminated on the Chameleon board but the blue/white light on the keypad is not, then the mem -
brane keypad and membrane keypad cable harness are suspect.
1. Disconnect the system from mains power.
2. Bridge pins 3 and 5 on X28 and connect main power.
3. If 48V green LED lights after power connected and system boots, replace the membrane keypad cable harness.
4. Boot without pins 3 and 5 bridged to check for normal operation.
5. If power is still not present, replace the membrane keypad.
317
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Change Fuses
There are two fuses located in the power cord receptacle at the rear of the supply unit. The primary symptom of fuse failure is an
inability to power on the laser unit. Fuses are present in both the SPM and CPD3 boards.
Remove Fuse Insert
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Before
starting any work power the laser system OFF and completely disconnected from mains power supply.
1. Remove the power cord from the power receptacle on the laser unit.
2. Pinch the tabs on both sides of the fuse insert (see Figure 9- 1 below ) and pull out the insert.
F IGURE 9- 1: Removing the fuse insert.
3. Remove the fuses and dispose of them according to the local regulations.
4. Insert new fuses (2 x T8A) into the insert.
318
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
5. Put the fuse insert back into the SPM/CPD.
6. Reinstall the power cord.
Failed Boot - MCIP Chameleon
l
Symptom: White light on Power/Standby button flashes as boot process starts, but does not stop flashing after 5 minutes or
more.
l
Symptom: When the Power/Standby button is pressed to start the boot process, the white light comes on solid, but does not
start flashing.
Multi- Core Chameleon Diagnostics
1. Did you wait long enough? There is an approximate lag time of 20 seconds before the white light starts to flash. Approx -
imately 5 minutes will elapse after the white light starts to flash until it remains on solidly.
2. Is the battery attached to the Chameleon board? Does the battery produce at least 2.7 volts (minimum voltage for boot)?
3. Are there 48V sent from Chameleon board to CP- Light?
l
Measure at X28.12 (48V) and X28.6 (Ground) during boot. If there is no voltage at X28.12, verify wiring at X28. If wir -
ing is correct, replace the Chameleon board. If voltage is present at X28, then proceed to step 4.
l
Measure 48V at power connector on CP- Light board during boot - If no voltage at CP- Light power connector replace
umbilical.
319
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
4. Unplug the laser system and insert a Rescue Stick USB drive with a rescue image file into USB port on Chameleon. This
image is more than just the CMark operating system.
l
If corrupted files on the Chameleon board are the issue causing boot to fail, overwriting the image will allow the
laser to boot after the rescue process is complete. It is necessary to connect to the Web Interface and load
CMark through the Web Interface if the system boots successfully.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symp -
tom of corrupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is
inserted into the USB port and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from
memory and overwritten with the factory default image. All customer files and CMark will be
lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Marking Head type/Lens/Serial
Number/IP addresses).
l
See "The Chameleon Rescue Stick" below for overview of Rescue Stick concept.
See "Create a Rescue Stick " on the facing page for detailed instructions on creating a Rescue Stick.
See "Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a Rescue Stick" on page 324 for detailed instructions on overwriting sys -
tem with Rescue Stick.
5. Try the rescue stick twice before replacing Chameleon. If a red Chameleon MCIP2 Artix is present in the laser system, you
must use rescue stick image PlexMark version 1.14. Older PlexMarks will not repair MCIP2 Artix. If the laser fails to boot fol -
lowing use of the Rescue Stick, replace the Chameleon board.
The Chameleon Rescue Stick
A Rescue Stick is a USB memory stick prepared with a CMark image that allows you to recover a failed laser. You can create a res -
cue stick and carry it with you as a service tool.
Minimum Required Version
CMark versions 4.0 and later support the Rescue Stick feature. You cannot recover single core Chameleon
boards with the rescue stick, only multi- core boards.
Use a Rescue Stick:
l
When a laser running CMark from the Flash memory will not boot.
l
When a laser will not finish the boot cycle, preventing you from reloading CMark via the Web Interface.
l
Before replacing the Chameleon to resolve failure to boot. A Rescue Stick will overwrite system files on the Chameleon,
allowing the laser to successfully boot if corrupted system files were the root cause.
It is suggested that you create a rescue stick before you need it and carry it as service tool. It takes approximately 45 minutes to
download the rescue image and write the files to USB.
When a prepared Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB slot on a Chameleon board, it will automatically overwrite anything in the
Chameleon Board Flash Memory. This will completely erase all files on Chameleon and set it to the manufacturer default
320
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
state with the exception of the system database . After this rewrite process, it is necessary to connect to the Web Interface to
load CMark. CMark is not included as part of the rescue image. After loading CMark, you should verify the Laser Configuration in
the Web Interface.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symptom of cor -
rupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB port
and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from memory and overwritten with the factory
default image. All customer files and CMark will be lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Mark -
ing Head type/Lens/Serial Number/IP addresses).
Create a Rescue Stick
The following procedure details installing the Rescue Image to a USB memory stick.
Rufus (The Reliable USB Formatting Utility with Source) is a free and open- source portable application for Microsoft Windows
that can be used to format and create bootable USB flash drives or Live USBs. Other software tools may also be used, but are
untested. It is important that a software tool is used to create a bootable drive and that you do not just copy and paste files onto
the USB drive.
Requirements for creating Rescue Stick
l
USB 4GB or larger
l
PlexMark 1.14 or newer (.iso file type) - Red Chameleon MCIP2 Artix boards require minimum PlexMark ver -
sion 1.14. Older versions of PlexMark are not supported by this board.
l
RUFUS 3.10 or newer, or other software capable of writing the Plexmark image to the USB drive. The fol -
lowing procedure uses RUFUS, but other software for creating bootable media may be used, though only
RUFUS has been tested.
1. Download both PlexMark 1.14.zip and Rufus 3.10.zip. Contact Videojet Technical Support if you do not have access to
the download locations.
VTI Technology & Support Team Space File Location
Service Resources/Service Software/Laser/Utility/Rescue Stick for Multicore Chameleon/Rescue Stick v 1.14
2. Unzip (extract) both the Plexmark 1.14 and Rufus folders.
3. Insert the USB drive into an open port on your PC.
Data Loss
All files on the USB drive will be deleted by this procedure.
4. Double click the Rufus executable (Application) to open the software application.
321
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
5. If you need to change the user interface language, click the Globe icon in the lower left and select the desired language
from the list.
6. Select your USB drive from the Device dropdown selector. If it is not listed, restart Rufus. Ensure the USB drive is inserted
before launching RUFUS so that the software discovers the USB drive.
7. Click Select and browse to the Plexmark.iso image file. When returned to the Drive Properties window, the file name
should display in the Boot selection field.

11. Wait until the Status Ready appears in green. Progress is shown during the writing process. The Rescue Stick is now

Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a Rescue Stick
This process only applies to Multi- Core Chameleon boards that will not successfully boot. This procedure will not recover a single
core Chameleon. Recovery of single core Chameleon boards that will not boot should be attempted through reloading of the SD
card with CMark or replacement of the SD card.
Laser Will Be Set Back to Factory Image
When a laser system is booted with a Rescue Image USB stick inserted into the USB slot on the Chameleon
board, it will wipe all files on the laser's flash storage. Those files are then irretrievable. The desired CMark must
be available for installation after Rescue Image is installed in order for the laser to function correctly.
The System Database, which is stored on EEPROM on Chameleon, will not be lost. This contains the laser type,
head type, lens, serial number and other hardware configuration items.
1. Insert the USB stick with the Rescue Image into USB port on the Chameleon board of the laser. For instructions on transfer
of Rescue Image to USB, see "Create a Rescue Stick " on page 321 .
2. Ensure the laser system is connected to power before proceeding. The blue light on the membrane keypad should be lit.
324
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
3. Press the On/Standby button on the membrane keypad of the laser to boot the laser. The white light will come on dimly
and eventually start to flash as the image on the USB memory stick begins to load. The laser system will shut down
when the image load is complete. Approximate time to completion is 5- 10 minutes.
If the Rescue Image loading was successful, the laser system should boot when powered on and will allow you to con -
nect to the Web Interface to continue. You must load CMark before checking the system configuration .
The only part of system that is not overwritten is the EEPROM. The EEPROM stores the system configuration including IP
addresses, laser type, head type, lens type, and serial number. Even though the EEPROM is not overwritten, check
these for accuracy after loading CMark or CMark Backup.
Troubleshooting: Laser still won't boot after second rescue
attempt
Always attempt rescue process a second time if laser still will not boot after first attempt.
Ensure if red MCIP2 Artix Chameleon is present that using PlexMark 1.14 as the rescue image. System
will not boot if older PlexMark is installed.
If the laser system still will not boot, replace the Chameleon board.
4. Install the desired CMark version or CMark Backup.
Installing a fresh install of CMark or a CMark backup are achieved through the same steps. For detailed information on
backup creation see "Firmware (CMark) Backup" on page 389 . Following are generalized steps for loading either CMark
or a CMark backup
Generalized steps to install CMark or CMark backup

4.1. Boot the laser.
4.2. Connect to the laser's Web Interface. Use Primary IP to connect (dip switch 1 in Off position). The IP previously set
by customer is not overwritten during the Rescue Stick process. If you are unsure of the primary IP Address, you can
connect to 192.168.1.1 with dip switch 1 in the On position to find the configured primary IP in the laser Web Inter -
face at Laser Menu | Configuration.
4.3. Set DIP Switch 1 to OFF if you used it to determine the primary IP Address.
4.4. Connect to the laser via the primary IP address.
The Web Interface branding will be Alltec Foba until after CMark is loaded.
4.5. Log in with the service level username and password.

4.6. Click on the Other menu and select CMark Update from the drop- down menu.
4.7. Browse to the desired CMark version or CMark Backup (if available) on your computer. After the CMark or CMark
backup loads, the laser will shut down.
5. Reboot the laser.
6. Connect to the Web Interface and check the Laser Configuration for accuracy. Following are generalized steps.
For more detailed information see "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 397 .
Generalized steps for checking/setting laser configuration
6.1. Boot the laser system.

then repeat the CMark Update process.

If no CMark Backup has been loaded, or if a newer Customer Database is available than was included in the CMark
Backup, then the Customer Database may be loaded using the appropriate user interface. If no backups are avail -
able to be installed, proceed to the desired user interface to create jobs/templates for laser marking.
Replacing Chameleon Board
The spare part Chameleon board does not come with the CMark operating system installed. You must load CMark and then set
the laser Configuration through the Web Interface before any other actions can be performed.
When a spare board arrives it is necessary to:
1. Move HIC and LIC from the bad board to new Chameleon board. The HIC and LIC do not come with Chameleon spare
part.
328
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
2. Boot and connect with the Web Interface. The Web Interface will display Alltec FOBA branding until after the CMark
Update.
3. Login with service username and password.
4. Upload CMark or CMark backup (Multi- Core Chameleon only) via the Web Interface. Red Chameleon MCIP2 Artix (AL-
84620) requires minimum CMark 4.15.1, while all previous Multi- Core Chameleons require CMark 4.1 or newer.
7340/7440 with MCIP2 Artix requires a minimum CMark version of 4.15.2 or newer, otherwise air pressure errors may
occur.
5. After the CMark Update is complete, reboot the laser.
6. Login with service username and password.

System Database is available it may be loaded through Other | System Database .
9. Click Save button.

Inherit Customer Data
Beginning with CMark 4.16, when replacing a Chameleon board it is possible to inherit the smarties folder from either an SD card
from a Single Core Chameleon or from a backup of a Multi- Core Chameleon. This process will allow you to (using PC) browse to
the "smarties" folder on an SD card taken from Single Core Chameleon or to an unzipped CMark backup (must be unzipped).
Loss of Data
The following procedure will overwrite all existing files on Chameleon in smarties/database (Templates, fonts,
logos, line setups, mark settings, etc.) and smarties/customer (SARs, AllPrintBasic files, etc.). This procedure
should only be used when replacing a Chameleon board or other instances where the desired outcome is to
overwrite the default data included with generic CMark version.
Tools required:
l
Customer's SD card from Single Core Chameleon or CMark backup from Multi- Core Chameleon
l
SD card reader (if inheriting from SD card)
l
PC
l
Ethernet cable
l
CMark 4.16 or newer (Feature exists since CMark 4.15, but only functions correctly since CMark 4.16)
Inherit Customer Data
1. Remove the Single Core Chameleon board, but retain the SD card from the removed board.
If replacing a Multi- Core Chameleon, create CMark backup if possible before proceeding and download to PC. Unzip the
CMark backup before continuing. See "CMark Backup" on page 454 for instructions on creating backup.
2. Install the new Chameleon board.
3. Boot the laser system.
4. Connect to the Laser Web Interface and log in.
330
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
5. Load CMark 4.16 or newer.
Allow Processes to Complete
Ensure the laser system is not performing BIOS updates before powering down. Do not shut down
laser until BIOS update is complete . A message with a countdown displays immediately after the
update if a BIOS update is applied.
6. Reboot the laser system when prompted.
7. Connect to the Web Interface and set the Laser Configuration or upload the System Database backup to the laser sys -
tem.
8. Reboot the laser system.
9. If applicable, take the SD card from Single Core board and insert it into the card reader on (or connected to) a PC.
10. Connect to the Web Interface and log in.
11. Select Other |  Customer Data from the dropdown menu. This feature is only functional in CMark 4.16 or newer. This
feature will overwrite all customer- specific data in CMark including jobs, fonts, logos, SARs, AllPrintBasic files, etc.
12. Select Choose File and browse to the SD card reader or other location on the PC or USB. If using data from a CMark
backup, ensure that the backup is unzipped.
331
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
13. Select the "smarties" folder.
14. Click the Upload button. Confirm that you would like to Upload when prompted.
The browser will start loading the smarties folder from the selected location on SD/PC/USB to the Chameleon board.
This can take several minutes depending on the number of files to be transferred. To the right of the Browse button the
number of files to be loaded is displayed. Below the Browse button are the names of the files to be loaded.
15. Reboot the laser when the "Upload finished" message displays.
16. Connect via TCS+ or SmartGraph to ensure that the customer data is present on the system.

Troubleshooting
No Communication with Marking Head
The following faults are considered marking head faults. The CP- Light board is neither transmitting nor receiving signals from the
galvos or the Chameleon board.
Symptom: Laser boots, but the following error occurs: No Communication with Marking Head error (E09100)
Diagnostics
1. Connect to the laser Interface and verify that the laser model and marking head type are set correctly.
If model and marking head type are correct continue to step 2.
If the model or marking head type are not correct:
1.1. Log in to the Web Interface
1.2. Change to the correct type.
1.3. Reboot the laser system after making the change.
2. Are the LEDs illuminated on the CP- Light board?
If the LEDs are illuminated, continue to step 3.
If no LEDs are not illuminated:
2.1. Check for 48V at the power connector on the CP- Light board.
2.2. If there is no voltage, verify 48V out at X28 pins 6 and 12.
3. Check the continuity on the LVDS cable (CAT- 7 cable) that runs between the HIC and the CP- Light. Ensure cable is inserted
completely into HIC_ CPA. You should hear and feel a click when pushed in and completely locked in place.
4. Ensure the marking head cable is securely connected at the galvos.
5. Check that the HIC board is inserted completely and Shutter Closed LED is on.
Marking Head Error - Temp High
The laser boots successfully, but the following error occurs: Marking Head: Temp of FM/MHI Assembly Group Too High
(E09000)
Warning generated at 62°C and error generated at 65°C.
Diagnostics
1. Verify that the supply unit cooling fan (small fan) is running. If it is not running, then the CP- Light board is likely over
temperature.
333
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
If the fan is not running, verify that it is connected to X28.1 and X28.2 on the Chameleon board and that the connector
between the fan and the Chameleon is fully seated. The fan receives 24V from this connection and should run all the
time as long as system is booted.
2. Check the Marking head temperature via TCS+ in the System Information screen to see if it is higher than 62 degrees.
3. To reduce temperature:
l
Lower duty cycle by increasing distance between products
l
Decrease jump speed
l
Improve cooling of marking head
Shutter Error
Shutter faults occur when the galvos do not close the mechanical shutter switch that identifies them as being in the closed pos -
ition. The faults that may occur are:
l
E10302 - Shutter does not stay open
l
E10303 - Shutter does not stay closed
334
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Overview
Shutter faults can be caused either by the galvos not moving to the position which would close the electromechanical shutter
(failure to move) or by the Chameleon not receiving the shutter closed information (failure to relay information).
Components
The following components are involved in the correct function of the shutter system.
l
CP- Light
l
Galvos
l
Marking Head Cable
l
HIC
l
Chameleon
Diagnostics
Possible Equipment Damage
When troubleshooting, only move galvos by hand when the system is in standby mode (blue light on keypad)
or when power to the system is completely OFF !
1. Verify that shutter and LVDS (CAT 7 cable) connectors are seated correctly at HIC and CP- Light boards.
2. Check continuity of shutter cable which runs from HIC to CP- Light. (Note: pink and gray wires merge on the HIC end of
cable, but are separate on CP- Light end).
3. Check that the connectors for the galvos at the CP- Light board and inside marking unit are seated securely and not dam -
aged.
4. Remove the marking head lens and cover.
5. With the unit off, gently move the galvos slightly from their current position.
6. Power the unit on and watch to see if the galvos move.
If there is no movement:
6.1. With the unit off, gently move the galvos slightly from their current position.
6.2. Power unit on and watch to see if the galvos move.
6.3. If there is still no movement or only movement on one galvo, replace the marking unit.
335
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
X or Y Mirror Errors
There are several voltages and signals required to move the mirrors in the marking head. The galvos provide feedback to the CP-
Light to maintain a closed feedback loop. When either output voltage or signals are not returned to the CP- Light from the galvos,
the following errors will occur:
l
Marking Head: Overpositioning of X- Mirror (E09002)
l
Marking Head: Overpositioning of Y- Mirror (E09003)
l
Marking Head: Overcurrent X- Mirror (E09004)
l
Marking Head: Overcurrent Y- Mirror (E09005)
l
No X Mirror Connected (E09006)
l
No Y Mirror Connected (E09007)
Diagnostics
Possible Equipment Damage
When troubleshooting, only move galvos by hand when the system is in standby mode (blue light on keypad)
or when power to the system is completely OFF !
1. Verify that the X and Y galvo voltage connectors from the CP- Light out to galvos are seated correctly.
2. Verify that the X and Y galvo position detect connectors (these carry feedback from galvos) are seated correctly on the CP-
Light.
3. Check the continuity of the marking head cable. If the marking head cable is damaged, then the laser source and umbilical
must be replaced. The marking head cable cannot be replaced independently.
4. With the unit off, gently move the galvos slightly from their current position.
5. Power the unit on and watch to see if the galvos move.
336
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
If there is no movement:
5.1. With the unit off, gently move the galvos slightly from their current position.
5.2. Power unit on and watch to see if the galvos move.
5.3. If there is still no movement or only movement on one galvo, replace the marking unit.
Exchanging Marking Head
You cannot replace any individual components within the marking unit except for wear parts such as o- rings and duckbill valve. If
faulty components such as galvos, internal mirrors, or lenses are suspected, then it is necessary to replace the entire marking unit.
If a different size marking field or spot size are needed, it is also necessary to change the entire marking unit.
l
Follow service instructions included with marking unit kit. The kit will include a CP- Light board and a USB drive with a Lens
Compensation file.
l
Do not install a marking unit without its paired CP- Light board. Do not forget to upload Lens Compensation file to the sys -
tem in TCS+. See "Loading the Lens Compensation File" on page 430 for details on loading and activating Lens Com -
pensation file.
Red Emissions LED Defective Error
(E00210)
There are two red Emissions lights, one on the membrane keypad of the supply unit and one at the junction of the umbilical and
marking head. Each must be functional, or the system will not enable laser power.
l
There are four LEDs embedded in the LEC board in the marking unit and all must be functional for the system to operate.
l
If any of the red emissions LEDs are not functioning, the following error will be displayed in the user interface: Red laser
emission LED defective (E00210)
Diagnostics
1. Check forward and backward resistance on the membrane keypad pins 2- 15. If open, replace the keypad.
2. Check the resistance between leads on the LEC board at the end of the umbilical inside the marking unit cover. If open,
replace the LEC board.
337
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Wrong HIC Detected Error
Symptom: Wrong HIC Detected Error (E00213) after boot.
Diagnostics
1. Ensure the HIC board is present and securely connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Connect to Web Interface and verify that model and marking head type are correct.
3. If the model and marking head set in the Web Interface are not correct, log in and change Configuration to the correct
laser type or marking head type.
This is a Fatal Error, so a complete reboot of system is required in order to clear error.
338
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Wrong LIC Detected Error
Symptom: Wrong LIC detected error (E00212).
Diagnostics
1. Determine the answer to the following questions:
l
Have the marking unit and CP- Light been replaced right before error occurred?
l
Were the CP- Light and marking used in any other laser before being installed here?
The CP- Light has code written upon boot based on the laser source and umbilical length. Once written, this code will
cause the laser to fault if used in any other laser if the laser source and umbilical length do not match the code written.
If the answer to either of the preceding questions is "yes", reset the code on the CP- Light board. See "Reset CP- Light
Code" below .
2. Ensure the X28 is seated securely at the Chameleon board.
3. Verify 24VDC to the Chameleon X28.13 to X28.14 (laser supply voltage).
4. Verify that the LIC is connected to the Chameleon board.
5. Connect to the Web Interface and select Laser | Configuration .
6. Verify that the Model Number and umbilical length are correct. Because the 7440 has a 3 meter and 10 meter umbil -
ical available, they are considered different model numbers in the laser configuration, i.e. 7440- 3m or 7440- 10m.
If the Model Number value is incorrect:
6.1. Log in to the Web Interface.
6.2. Select Laser | Configuration .
6.3. Choose the correct model number from the dropdown selector.
7. Check if the error is cleared. If not, continue troubleshooting.
8. Verify that both ribbon cables from the LIC are securely connected to the laser source. Note that you must remove the
lower rear panel to see the connectors, and may need to loosen screws and slide the source out through the back slightly to
access the connectors.
Reset CP- Light Code
When a CP- Light board is installed in a laser, a code is written to the CP- Light during the first time the laser boots after install -
ation of the board. This code is specific to the laser model detected from the Laser Configuration on Chameleon. Once this code
is written, the CP- Light cannot be used in another model of laser. CMark 4.7 and newer allows this code to be reset in TCS+ in
cases where the CP- Light (and its paired galvo set/marking unit) are moved to a laser of a different type. Prior to CMark 4.7, this
code could not be rewritten.
339
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Never Separate Galvos and CP- Light
The CP- Light board is paired in manufacturing with a galvo set and calibrated for best positional accuracy
based on the physical characteristics of the galvo set. This calibration is saved to the CP- Light sent with the
galvo set. Do not separate these pieces. Install both CP- Light and galvo set together. If separated, poor print
quality or errors may occur. For 7340/7440 an even higher degree of accuracy is required and a lens com -
pensation file is included with the parts. This file must be installed through TCS+ or the Web Interface after
the parts are installed to the laser.
Reset CP- Light Code
1. Connect to laser via TCS+ and login as a Service User. Only Service User can reset a CP- Light code. Service User requires the
daily password.
2. Press the Main Menu button in the Home page.
3. Press the Laser Unit Settings option in the dropdown menu.
4. Ensure Laser Configuration is selected in the left pane.
5. Press the Reset CP- Light button at the top of the window on the right.
340
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
6. Press Yes to continue when prompted or No to cancel the process.
When the process is complete, a dialog box will display that indicates the reset was successful.
7. Press the OK button.
8. Press the Close button in Laser Unit Settings. You will be required to reboot laser. During this boot, the CP- Light code will be
written based on the model set in Laser Configuration.

General Error

Diagnostics
"General Error" is often a sign of a bad laser source, but check the following before replacing the laser source:
1. Check for 24V at Terminal Block and back of laser source.
2. Ensure the serial interface cable securely connected at back of laser source (small ribbon cable).
3. Ensure the digital interface cable securely connected at back of laser source (large ribbon cable).

ectly out of the collimator to determine if low power is due to laser source or due to components in marking unit blocking the
laser beam.

Safety Preparation
Before proceeding with the power measurement, read, understand, and implement the following safety precautions.
343
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
Direct or diffuse laser radiation can inflict severe bodily damage , including, but not limited to, permanent eye
damage or blindness, and severe burns to human or animal tissue.  Always wear the appropriate eye pro -
tection when in the same area as an operating laser. Do not allow a direct or reflected beam to contact a per -
son .
Diligence and awareness are essential to prevent the beam from traveling beyond the intended work area.
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
When performing a power measurement, it is the responsibility of the person performing the power meas -
urement to  enclose and protect the surrounding area and personnel from the laser beam and, when possible,
perform the power measurement within the laser enclosure / beam shield.
Warning - Serious Personal Injury
When performing a power measurement, the use of a Carbon Block is required , placed behind the power
meter to absorb the laser energy when misalignments occur.

Prepare the Laser Unit, Meter and Area for the Power Meas -
urement
Eye and Skin Hazard
Direct or diffuse laser radiation can inflict severe bodily damage, including, but not limited to, permanent eye
damage or blindness and severe burns to human or animal tissue. Always wear appropriate eye protection
when in the same area as an operating laser. Do not allow a direct or reflected beam to contact a person.
Always be aware of the laser’s beam path when using a laser power meter and always use a beam block to pre -
vent the beam from traveling beyond the intended work area.
1. Ensure that all personnel in the area are wearing the appropriate protective eye wear.
2. Ensure the exposed laser beam will remain enclosed during the test.
l
If the laser application already uses an enclosure, perform the test with the laser head inside its enclosure.
l
If the laser is not enclosed already in the application, place non flammable materials around the laser head to
enclose it during the test. Use materials appropriate for blocking the wavelength in use. See the training mater -
ials for this model for details on appropriate enclosure materials by wavelength.
3. Place the carbon block below the marking head in the beam path, preferably horizontally below the downward aimed mark -
ing head. If the application is a straight shot, use prop the carbon block in front of the beam path.
346
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
4. Position the target of the power meter between the marking head and carbon block. The carbon block's purpose is to
absorb the beam in cases where the beam misses the target.
5. Position the marking head such that the head is  not  at the working focal distance - preferably the distance should be a
quarter to a third of the actual working focal distance if you cannot safely use the following distances.
Air Knife Effect - Measurement Distance
The positive air that cools the marking unit and acts as an air knife at the marking unit output can con -
tribute to erroneous readings by cooling the power meter sensor. The recommended distance from
power meter sensor is as follows:
Marking Head Distance from Power Meter Sensor
CFS/CFT - Small 350mm
CFS/CFT - Medium 400mm
CFS/CFT - Large 450mm

range (.8 - 6 is in microns, which equates to 800 - 6,000 nanometers).

Troubleshooting
l
If power output from marking unit output was low but straight out of collimator the power is acceptable, replace the mark -
ing unit. Assumption here is that something is blocking beam within the marking unit such as lens or mirror that has come
loose.
l

1. Ensure Mark Settings were correct (Intensity 100% and Frequency 30,000 Hz).
2. If the mark settings where correct, replace the laser source.
Perform the Leakage Tests
There are two different leakage tests to perform.
l
High power – requires the use of a power meter.
l
Low power – requires the use of thermo- reactive paper
Disable Leakage Protection
1. Login into the Laser Web Interface.
2. Select Laser | Config .
3. Click to toggle Leakage Protection Enabled to OFF .
4. Click Save to save your changes.
Perform the High Power Test
1. Ensure the key switch is in the open position.
2. Leave the power meter in position as it was for the power test.
3. Close the key switch and start printing a template which requires a trigger signal for printing but do not trigger the laser .
4. While system is Waiting for Trigger mode, monitor the power meter for the duration (1 minute).
5. Allow the system to idle 1 additional minute waiting for a Trigger before continuing on to another test.
6. Stop marking.
7. Open the key switch to disable marking.
354
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Perform the Low Power Test
1. Ensure the key switch is in the open position.
2. Leave the power meter in position as it was for the power test.
3. Place a piece of thermo- reactive paper on top of the power meter.
4. Close the key switch and start printing a template which requires a trigger signal but do not trigger the laser .
5. For up to 1 minute while system is in Waiting for Trigger mode, check for burn marks or discoloration on the thermo- react -
ive paper.
6. Stop marking.
7. Open the key switch to disable marking.
Leakage Test Results and Next Actions
Test Pass Fail Corrective Action if Failed
High 0 Watts output during test  > 0 Watts output during Test and replace the ACC- 3, SC Chameleon, or
Power test MC Chameleon and LIC board
Low No marks or burning on Discoloration, marks, Test and replace the laser source.
Power thermo- reactive paper or burns on paper
Test Completion Safety Requirement
When the power measurement is complete, delete the power check parameter set or zero the marking intensity.
 Ensure Power Measurement Template is No Longer Selected
Before leaving the laser ensure that Power Measurement template is not the active template in Laser Oper -
ation tab!! Leaving Power Measurement selected will cause laser to automatically fire the laser, endangering
unaware users.

Laser Not Ready Error (E00200)
Symptom: Laser Not Ready Error (E00200)
Diagnostics
1. Verify 24VDC at Terminal block 1 and 2.
2. Ensure that wires connecting the laser power supply to the terminal block and the wires going out from the terminal block
to the source are seated correctly and not on insulation.
3. Check the power connector at the rear of the laser source.
4. Check the serial connection (small ribbon cable) to the laser source. When the key switch is turned, the Chameleon board
looks for info from the source right away.
5. Check the DB25 connector (big ribbon cable) to the laser source. When the key switch is turned, the Chameleon board
looks for info from the source right away.
356
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Defective Element in Laser Supply
Error
The system must sense 24 Volts from the laser power supply at three places. Voltage from the laser power supply must be
returned to the X28 connector on the Chameleon board and the X9 connector on the SPM or CPD board. If voltage is not
returned to X28 and X9, the following fault will occur: Defective Element in Laser Supply (E00211). If voltage is not sensed at
laser source then Laser Supply Voltage Too Low/High will assert.
Diagnostics
1. Verify that latching arms on X9 connector of SPM/CPD and arms on X28 connector on Chameleon are completely locked
down.
2. Verify that X9 pin 3 has the red wire (laser power) inserted completely and that X9 pin 13 has the gray (laser ground) inser -
ted completely.
3. Verify 24V is present to X9 pin 3 and ground at X9 pin 13. These wires can be difficult to seat because of their thickness. If
no voltage is read, try reinserting the wires.
UADB Errors
Symptom: Wrong air pump assembly position (E00312) or The connection to the air pump orientation sensor is lost (E00311).
UADB Discontinued December 2021
The UADB board was discontinued in December 2021 and all 7340/7440 laser units manufactured after that
time will not have UADB, nor the errors associated with the board. All 7340/7440 laser units without UADB
board must use CMark 4.12.1 or newer or errors will occur when the board is not detected.
If UADB board errors occur on units still fitted with UADB, recommended action is to remove the UADB board
and to update CMark to version 4.12.1 or newer.
Diagnostics
1. Mount the air pump assembly according to supply unit orientation. If supply unit is to be mounted on its side, the com -
pressor must be rotated to use alternative mounting holes.
357
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 9- 5: Alternative mounting holes used to mount the compressor when the supply unit will be mounted vertically.
F IGURE 9- 6: Proper mounting position of the compressor for horizontal supply unit orientation.
358
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 9- 7: Proper compressor orientation when supply unit is mounted on its side (vertical).
2. Update CMark to version 4.12.1 or newer. The UADB is no longer monitored beginning with CMark 4.12.1. The board and
its cable may be removed as well.
3. If CMark cannot be updated to 4.12.1 then relocate UADB board to alternate position such as attached to top of laser
source with double sided tape. UADB board is sensitive to vibration from compressor and may cause intermittent E00312
Wrong Air Pump Assembly Position errors.
UADB board and cable are no longer available as spare parts. If either cable or board fails then the CMark must be
updated.
EtherNet/IP, PROFINET Laser Statuses Changes (CMark 4.9 +)
Update from CMark 4.7.6 to CMark 4.9 or newer contains changes to EtherNet/IP and PROFINET status
responses from laser. Please advise customer's PLC programmer of these changes if updating CMark from
4.7.6 to newer CMark if laser is using EtherNet/IP or PROFINET.
Interlock Open - Troubleshooting
The laser system uses a safety circuit that requires two circuits for the Interlock system to both be closed in order to enable the
laser power. If one of these two circuits is open, the Interlock Open condition cannot be cleared.
Symptom: Interlock Open status in user interface and no red LEDs on marking head or supply unit keypad.
359
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Diagnostics
1. Verify both interlock circuits are closed.
2. Check the wiring at the door switch/emergency switch on the customer side (no loose wires).
3. Verify the connection at X9 pins 17- 21 and 15- 19.
4. Check the fuse at X9.
5. If no FASS is installed:
5.1. Verify that the jumper from X9 pins 1- 23 is in place or that a reset switch is installed.
5.2. If single pole switch is used, ensure that jumpers have been moved so that both channels of Interlock see open and
close. (Switch from 15- 19 and jumpers from 15- 17 and 19- 21). See "Single Pole Wiring SPM/CPD" on page 169 .
5.3. If a reset switch is installed, ensure that it is normally open.
5.4. If a reset switch is installed, ensure that it closes and opens following reset.
If a FASS is installed:
5.1. Ensure the reset switch is properly connected to connector X9 on pins 1- 23.
5.2. Ensure the reset switch is normally open.
5.3. Ensure the switch closes and opens again following interlock open state.
6. Verify that both interlock circuits open and close at same time. Too much time between open and close on circuit 1 and 2
results in the Interlock Open warning. A double pole switch is always recommended.
7. Verify SPM/CPD functionality:
7.1. Power down the laser.
7.2. Remove the customer wiring to X9 on the SPM/CPD board.
7.3. Install jumpers to the X9 as described in Table 9- 4 below .
7.4. Power on the laser and check if the Interlock Open warning has cleared.
7.5. If Interlock Open does not clear with the jumpers in place, replace the SPM board or CPD3 board.
Table 9- 4: Jumper Config to test SPM/CPD
Jumper
Signal
Pins
5- 16 Door 2 (Shutterlock)
7- 14 Door 1 (Shutterlock)
10- 12 Door Feedback
15- 19 Emergency 2 (Interlock)
17- 21 Emergency 1 (Interlock)
22- 24 Emergency Feedback
1- 23 Interlock Release - SPM and SPM- 16A Only (Not used with CPD, CPD+FASS or SPM-
16A+FASS)
360
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Clicking Noise When Interlock Is Open
The laser system opens and closes two relays on the CPD when any doors connected to the two interlock circuits are open. These
two circuits are meant to be redundant and to be used with a double pole switch. When only one circuit is used and the other cir -
cuit has jumper left in place, one of the relays struggles to stay closed. Over time, this will eventually damage the board.
Symptom: Rhythmic clicking noise can be heard from supply unit while Interlock Open.
Remove Jumpers/Bypasses When Work is Complete
Bypassing any interlocks or the key switch places the user in a dangerous situation that can result in laser radi -
ation exposure, skin damage, and eye damage. Ensure connections you jumper for troubleshooting are
properly configured when work is complete .
Solution
If using a single pole switch, use the wire the switch to X9 as shown in Figure 9- 8 below to avoid long term damage.
F IGURE 9- 8: Wiring for a single pole switch to XP on the CPD board.
On the X9 connector of CPD:
1. Remove the jumper from 15- 19.
2. Wire the single pole switch between pins 15 and 19 .
3. Remove the default jumper from 17- 21.
4. Place a jumper from 15- 17.
5. Place a jumper from 19- 21.
361
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
MIC Not Found Error
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
When installed, the MIC board must be enabled through the Web Interface. If the Web Interface settings do not match the pres -
ence of the MIC, then errors will occur.
Symptom: The Miscellaneous Interface Controller (MIC) was not found or is not supported (E15100).
Diagnostics
l
Verify the MIC board connected to Chameleon if it installed in the system.
l
Verify that Web Interface | Other | External Interface is toggled to Disabled if no MIC is installed.
Wrong MIC Detected Errors
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
There are two different versions of MIC board. There is one MIC board for EtherNet/IP and one MIC board for PROFINET. The
boards contain the firmware specific to that version and this cannot be changed in the field.
Symptoms
l
The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. EtherNet/IP module not found. (E15101)
l
The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. PROFINET module not found. (E15102)
Diagnostics
1. Verify the MIC board is connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Ensure that the correct MIC is installed (Blue dot for EtherNet/IP or Yellow for PROFINET).
3. In the Web Interface, select Other and verify that Interface is set to the correct MIC option (EtherNet/IP or PROFINET)
for the installed MIC.
362
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
Waiting for Trigger Status
Symptom: Laser status displays "Waiting for Trigger" and no mark is made.
Diagnostics
1. Verify the Start button has been pressed.
2. Check that interlocks are closed.
3. Ensure that the correct Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity) is selected.
4. Verify that the Trigger Enable input X25.9 has 24V applied or is jumpered to X25.12. Voltage is necessary to X25.9 for
trigger signals to be acknowledged.
Web Interface Set to Trigger Enable Voltage Needed
If Trigger Enable Voltage needed is set to Default (3x40) or to True and 24V is not applied to X25.9
then Waiting for Trigger status will appear and no mark will be allowed. This condition applies when
using Product Registration/Line Setup is set to an external source (such as a photo eye, proximity
switch, or trigger from a PLC). Ensure that 24V is applied to X25.9 Trigger Enable input in order to avoid
constant Waiting for Trigger condition.
5. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag product detector. If LED does not flash verify wiring and
exchange product detector.
No Mark on Product
Symptom: Product and Mark Count increase, but no mark is observed on product.
363
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Diagnostics
1. Clean the output lens.
2. Verify working distance is correct for the lens fitted to marking head.
3. Adjust marking head so that it is perpendicular to product.
4. Remove any obstructions in beam path such as conveyor guards or fume extraction nozzles.
5. Verify Parameter settings are correct. Increase Intensity setting.
6. Verify Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity Laser Controller). Are correct Trigger source and Start
Delay selected?
7. Attempt to mark with no product movement. Change product registration to one with No Product Movement and First
Trigger set to Auto and Consecutive Trigger set to None (SmartGraph) or Internal Once (TCS/Clarity). If laser marks
product with no product movement and internal trigger source then laser is capable of marking and problem is with
Product Registration or Parameters.
Attach Product Registration in SmartGraph
Ensure that after creating a Product Registration in SmartGraph you also attach the Product Regis -
tration to the Template in the Template tab. Changes to a Product Registration only take effect with a
Template if attached through Template tab.
8. Verify D20 LED (Trig Connected) on the Chameleon is lit if using product detect. LED is lit if Product Detector is sensed by
Chameleon. The system will report an error if Trigger Rising/Falling (SmartGraph) or External Rising/External Falling
(TCS/Clarity) is selected in the user interface. Voltage and Ground for the product detector must be attached to X25.4
(Voltage) and X25.8 (ground) or the No Trigger Detector Connected error asserts.
9. Verify D16 LED (Encoder Connected) on the Chameleon is lit (if using encoder). LED is lit if encoder is sensed by the
Chameleon. The system reports an error if Encoder is selected in the user interface and no encoder is sensed. The encoder
supply line must be connected to X25.2 and ground to X25.6 or No Encoder Detected error is asserted.
10. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag the product detector. If the LED does not flash, verify wir -
ing and exchange the product detector.
364
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
F IGURE 9- 9: Chameleon board LEDs related to Trigger and Encoder functions.
Poor Print Quality
Symptom: Mark quality is unacceptable.
First steps in troubleshooting poor print quality
l
Clean output lens.
See "Protective Glass Cleaning Procedure" on page 243 .
l
Verify and if necessary correct working distance.
See "Marking Fields & Working Distances" on page 146 .
l
Ensure marking head is as perpendicular as possible to area on product being marked.
Quality Unacceptable, Correct Spacing
Is issue with entire code (faint or scorched)?
l
Yes, the whole code is faint or scorched - Adjust Marking Speed or Marking Intensity in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark
Settings (TCS, Clarity)
l
No, only parts of characters are affected - Proceed to next step
365
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
Is the issue with beginning of stroke (use Show Jumps button in mark field toolbar in SmartGraph to show marking order)?
l
Yes, the issue is at beginning of stroke - adjust Jump Delay of Jump Speed is waves/squiggles at beginning of stroke or Laser
On- Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings (TCS, Clarity Laser Controller).
l
Yes, issue at end of stroke - adjust Stroke Delay or Laser Off- Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings (TCS, Clar -
ity Laser Controller).
l
Yes, curved portions of letters wider than straight portions - adjust Mark Delay in Parameters (SmartGraph) or Mark Settings
(TCS, Clarity Laser Controller).
Is product wet or covered in ice?
l
Water droplets and ice crystals refract the laser energy. Use an air knife to dry product area or move laser to different place
on production line where product will not be wet or ice covered.
Has the product changed?
l
Changes in the product material or the manufacturing process can affect Parameter settings necessary to mark product or
make product unable to be marked.
Does the product contain calcium carbonate?
l
Calcium carbonate reflects the CO2 wavelength and makes marking the material difficult or impossible.
Crooked Code, Characters Overlap, Misshapen Letters
Is Mounting Position/Orientation of marking head correct in software?
If an encoder is connected, use Kompensation Template to verify Encoder Distance per Rotation and Angle of Laser and Dir -
ection of Product Movement are correct. In TCS/Clarity, verify line direction is correct.
See "Kompensation Template" on page 185 for more information on Kompensation template.
If using Fixed Speed line setup, ensure the speed set in software matches the actual conveyor speed.
Examples of incorrect encoder and product movement settings follow:
366
Rev AC

Troubleshooting
367
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
SmartGraph Cannot Connect To Laser
Symptom: When attempting to connect to the laser using SmartGraph software, you receive the error, "Cannot Connect to Laser".
1. Verify that the laptop/PC IP address is set to work with the IP address of the laser system port. The first three octets of
the laptop/PC address must match that of the laser and the last octet must be different than that of the laser. Example:
The laser ACC board IP address is 192.168.1.1 so then the PC IP address must be 192.168.1._ _ (number 0- 255 other
than "1").
If the IP address of the laser is unknown:
1.1. Move DIP switch 1 to the ON position.
1.2. Restart the laser system. The laser IP will now be set to default IP of 192.168.1.1.
1.3. Set your PC to use an IP address compatible with 192.168.1.1.
2. Connect to the Web Interface. If you can connect to the Web Interface, a connection from SmartGraph is possible and
the issue is likely a version conflict between the SmartGraph version installed on your PC and the CMark version installed
on the laser.
3. Verify the version of CMark installed on laser and its required version of SmartGraph needed:
3.1. Connect to the laser Web Interface.
3.2. Select Log | Boot Log .
3.3. Determine the version of SmartGraph required.
3.4. Ensure you have installed the correct version on your PC.
4. If you still cannot connect, then the issue is with the connection itself.
4.1. Verify the Ethernet cable is in serviceable condition. You should be using a crossover cable, however most modern
PC/Laptop network adapters can auto switch to crossover mode for you.
4.2. Check for activity at the Ethernet status lights on Ethernet ports on Chameleon board.
4.3. Verify that the PC IP address is set on the correct network connection.
4.4. Boot the laser and connect Ethernet cable.
4.5. On the PC, select Start | Control Panel | Network and Sharing Center | Change Adapter Settings.
4.6. Look for the LAN connection that shows "Unidentified Network".
4.7. Unplug the Ethernet cable to the laser and verify the status changes to "Network Cable Unplugged."
4.8. If you see the status change for a different LAN connection, switch your troubleshooting to that connection.
368
Rev AC

TCS+
This user interface can be accessed via any supported web browser connected to the same network as the laser, or using a
TU440/TU430 touchscreen monitor. Many of the features covered here also apply to the legacy TCS software, which was avail -
able on single core Chameleon laser units. You will see it noted here where features not available in the legacy TCS interface are
described.
TU440 Overview
The TU440 is a touchscreen for use only with TCS+ running on multi- core Chameleon boards. There is no processing on board
the TU440, so the unit cannot run the legacy, PC- based versions of TCS software nor any other PC- based software. The TU440 is
a direct replacement for the previous TU430 model, but the TU440 uses all new parts including a new display.
Features of TU440:
l
Two USB ports (left side of unit)
l
Connection to 3210 via specialized HDMI connection
l
IP65 rating
l
4 meter cable length
F IGURE 10- 1: TU440 with the USB ports cover in place on the left side of the touchscreen.
369
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
F IGURE 10- 2: TU430 touchscreen (discontinued).
There are two USB ports located on the TU440 usable for file transfer or connecting a USB mouse. A USB keyboard can be con -
nected to the TU440 on lasers running CMark versions older then version 4.5. CMark version 4.5 and newer will not recognize a
USB keyboard.
The USB cover rotates for access to the ports.
F IGURE 10- 3: USB port cover in the open position.
Laser units connect to the TU440 through a specialized HDMI connector and cable which carry both signal and power for the
touchscreen.

TCS+
Possible Equipment Damage - Powered HDMI Port
Never plug a standard monitor into the laser HDMI port as voltage at the HDMI port will damage the monitor.
TU440 is a passive display to interface with the TCS+ program running on the Multi- Core Chameleon board. You can calibrate
the TU440/TU430 touchscreen via the TCS+ software. See "Display Settings TU4x0" on page 375 .
There are two versions of the TU440 as there were for the TU430:

Cable and Connectors
The cable for the TU440 is replaceable, but note that it does not utilize a quick disconnect at the screen side. The cable connector
has an IP65 housing for use with 3140/3340/3640/7230/7330 laser units. The cable connector fits into a connector on the rear
of 3140/3340/3640 and 7230/7330/7340/7440 supply units.
F IGURE 10- 5: (Left) Cable from TU440 with IP65 housing installed. (Right) Connector for TU440/TU430 on supply unit of 3x40
laser.
The IP65 housing is incompatible with 7510/7610/7810 lasers as the connector fits directly onto a connector on the Chameleon.
The IP65 cover prevents the cable from fully inserting to the connector on the board. The AL- 82785 part number for TU440 is
specific to 7510/76107810 fiber and UV lasers. There is no housing on the connector and the cable is stripped so that applying a
cable tie to hold the stripped portion against the grounding bar inside a 7510/7610/7810 supply unit creates a shield ground.

TCS+
F IGURE 10- 6: HDMI connection on 7510/7610/7810.
Hot Plugging the TU440/TU430
So long as no external USB memory drive is plugged into the TU440/TU430, you may hot plug or unplug the unit from the laser
at any time. However, if a USB drive is plugged into the TU440/TU430 hot plugging may cause file corruption on the
USB memory drive. While a USB memory drive is inserted, only plug/unplug the TU440/TU430 connector at the laser when the
system is off or in Standby mode.

are not replaceable on their own. Damage to these components requires a replacement of the entire unit. Spare parts for the
TU430 are still available, but the entire unit can no longer be ordered. If an entire unit needs to be replaced then a TU440 must
be ordered.

Focus Finder Activation
The 7340/7440 laser system is equipped with a pilot laser built into the laser source. This focus finder can help you to find the cor -
rect focal distance for marking by rapidly and alternately projecting two images, a circle and a dot, which appear to the human
eye simultaneously. As the marking head approaches the correct focal distance, the dot moves closer to the circle. At the correct
distance, the dot should appear centered inside the circle.
The Focus Finder feature can only be activated through TCS+ when you are logged in at a level that has access to edit the Line
Setup. Not all users are allowed to access this option by default.
Wear appropriate eye protection
Use of the Focus Finder feature requires that the laser source is armed. Though the pilot laser used in the
Focus Finder is Class II, when the laser source is armed it cannot be guaranteed that there is no Class IV beam
of wavelength 1,055- 1,075 escaping. Therefore eye protection for the 1,055- 1,075 wavelength should be
worn or the marking head should be in approved enclosure whenever the Focus Finder or pilot laser are in use.
Activation of Focus Finder
Activation differs depending on whether the laser has CMark version 4.5 or CMark 4.9+.
CMark version 4.9 or newer
To activate the focus finder option:
378
Rev AC

TCS+
1. Connect to TCS+ either via a web browser or with a TU4x0.
2. Login as a user with Access Rights to enable the Focus Finder (Admin or Service by default).
Granting Rights to Other Users
To grant access to Users, the Admin must give the Role access to use the Pilot Laser in Roles and Access
Rights | Operation. All Users with that Role will then be able to activate/deactivate the Focus Finder.
3. Press the Main Menu button in the Home screen.
4. Press the Pilot Laser option in the dropdown menu displayed.
5. Close the Keyswitch if not already closed. The keyswitch must be closed for the Focus Finder to function.
379
Rev AC

Avoid Prolonged Unnecessary Use of Focus Finder Mode
The method used to provide the Focus Finder feature can tax the galvos if used for prolonged periods
of time. Do not leave the Focus Finder mode on unnecessarily for prolonged periods of time to avoid
accelerated wear and tear on galvos.
7. Press the Stop Pilot Laser button to stop the Focus Finder at any time.
8. Press Close button to exit and return to Home screen.

Backup & Restore
You can use the File Management option in TCS to create a backup of the complete laser database, which includes all TCS jobs,
fonts, line setups, and marking sets. This backup also includes configuration settings for TCS+ including Users and Access Rights.
By default, the backup file name is written as the IP address of the laser and the backup date. For example, if the laser IP is
192.168.1.1 and the backup was created on May 27, 2017, the file name will be IP_ 192.168.1.1_ backup_ 05_ 27_ 2017.

2. Touch File Management . The File Management screen will open.
3. Touch Complete Laser Image on the Laser Local (left side) of File Management window. The number next to Complete
Laser Image refers to the number of files stored in the image.
4. Touch the Pen next to Complete Laser Image .

vidually. To import/export only individual files to/from TCS, use the File Import and Export option.

USB.

Firmware (CMark) Update
Using CMark version 4.7 or newer, you can update CMark operating system using TCS+ via web browser. CMark can never be
loaded or backed up using TU440/TU430 due to extremely slow speeds on USB ports.
Spare part Chameleons or Chameleon where Rescue Stick has been used do not contain CMark, but rather only PlexMark which a
manufacturer level software that allows laser to boot and user to access Web Interface to load desired CMark.
l
PlexMark (Rescue Stick) version 1.14 or newer allows CMark to be loaded via Web Interface or using TCS+ via web
browser.
l
PlexMark (Rescue Stick) version 1.7 only allows CMark to be loaded via Web Interface. This PlexMark does not have access
to TCS+ until after CMark is loaded.
Restoring Backups
TCS+ with CMark 4.7 or newer allows you to backup and restore your CMark via TCS+. Backup restoration is
the same process as updating, except that you choose a backup file instead of an update file. For details on
how to create a CMark backup see "Firmware (CMark) Backup" on page 389 .
To update CMark software using TCS+ you must:
1. Update Firmware (CMark)
2. Restart the laser
Required Items
l
Ethernet crossover cable
l
CMark software saved locally (SAR version if applicable)
l
(When applicable) A previous CMark backup - multi- core Chameleon only
386
Rev AC

TCS+
Update Firmware (CMark)
1. Connect to the laser via TCS+ as a user with sufficient Access Rights to update Firmware. By default, only the Service
user has Access Rights to this function. The Service user can assign this right to other users.
Must Connect via Primary IP with Dip Switch 1 Off
TCS+ functions that upload to or download from the laser require that you connect to the primary
IP address set in Main Menu/Laser Unit Settings/Network menu .
l
You cannot upload to or download from the laser when connected via the default IP with Dip Switch 1
in the On position if the primary IP configured is different from that default.
l
It is also not possible to upload/download using the secondary IP (if it exists).
l
Only the primary IP with Dip Switch 1 in Off position will allow for functions with file transfer in TCS+
or in the laser Web Interface.
2. Select File Management from the Main Menu in the Home screen.
3. Click Upload in the File Management window to select the .zip file containing the target version of CMark. The Open
dialog window will open. It does not matter what type of file is selected in the File Management window. Based on the
type of file that you select, the system will put the file in the appropriate location on the laser.
4. Brwose to the directory in which the file is stored.
387
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
5. Click to select the desired file.
6. Click Open . You will be returned to the previous window and a progress bar will show upload progress. This is only for the
upload of the CMark or backup into RAM. There are additional steps needed before the CMark on the system will be
overwritten.
After the system acknowledges the file, the Firmware Update screen will appear and you can choose whether to main -
tain the customer's database.
7. As appropriate, toggle Keep Customer Data and select the Customer Data Source.
l
If Keep Customer Data is toggled on, the Database containing templates, fonts, graphics, etc. will be backed up and
then reloaded onto the card as part of the update process.
l
The system will also maintain any AllPrint Basic and TCL files loaded on the laser system without any additional steps
for backup.
8. Click the Start button to begin the upgrade process and allow the process to complete. Clicking Cancel will abort the
upgrade process.
The update process may take up to 10 minutes. Be patient.
When process is completed screen will show the message "Done" system will go into Standby mode.
BIOS Update
Following some CMark updates it may sometimes be necessary to update the BIOS on the system. This
is done automatically following some CMark updates. It is very important not to interrupt this pro -
cess! ! The user interface will display a message with a timer counting down from 5 minutes and indic -
ating you should not disconnect power during this update. If you disrupt power during the BIOS
update, the Chameleon is no longer usable and will need to be replaced . After the BIOS update
is finished a message will indicate that it is necessary to disconnect power. The keypad power button
(Start/Standby) is inoperable during and after the BIOS update. It is therefore necessary to completely
power down the system by either turning off main power switch on rear of supply unit or by unplug -
ging system.
388
Rev AC

TCS+
9. Press the Power/Standby button to restart the system.
10. Reconnect to TCS+ after boot process is complete. Login as any User.
11. Press Videojet logo button in upper right of Home screen.
12. Select the About button in from the drop- down and verify that the correct version of CMark is loaded.
Firmware (CMark) Backup
It is good practice to occasionally create a backup of the CMark stored on the laser system. This backup will include not only the
CMark operating system, but also the customer database (Jobs, Fonts, Logos, etc.) and TCS+ interface with its User information
(configured Users, Roles, Access Rights, Passwords, etc.). This backup can be loaded if file corruption is suspected on the system or
if a Chameleon board needs to be replaced. Also included in the backup are any SAR/TCL files that have been loaded to the sys -
tem, AllPrintBasic files, and Lens Compensation files. These files as well as TCS+ jobs or job elements can also be backed up sep -
arately through the File Management tab if desired.
CMark version 4.7 or newer
Firmware backup function only exists in CMark version 4.7 and newer.
File Creation and Download Time
Creating the backup can take 20- 30 minutes because of the size of the file. It is not advised to interrupt the
backup process.
Create a Firmware Backup
1. Connect and log in to TCS+ as User with access rights to create backup. By default, the Administrator and Service level
can create backups, though these Users can delegate rights to other Users in Roles/Access Rights.
TU440/TU430 Not Recommended for Backup Creation
It is not recommended to create backup using TU440/TU430 port, as the speed of these ports is
extremely slow and the length of time required for backup creation could be confused for failure. If
using a TU440/TU430 touchscreen, insert a USB drive with at least 500 MB of free space into the
TU440/TU430 USB port. If using a web browser on PC, then it is not necessary to use USB.
2. Press the Main Menu button.
389
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
3. Select File Management from the drop- down menu.
4. Scroll down to Firmware option in the window.
5. Press the Pen button.
6. Press the Download button.
Download Will Take 20- 30 Minutes
Be patient as the creation and download of the Firmware backup will take considerable time. Before pressing
Download button be aware that process will take 20- 30 minutes. There is no progress bar.
Immediately after pressing the Download button, the system will create a backup of CMark firmware and when complete will
download to either USB drive in folder called TCS if using TU440/TU430 touch screen (not recommended) or into Downloads
folder on PC when using TCS+ via web browser (depending on web browser settings).
When the download is complete the TCS+ screen will return to File Management menu. You can now use PC to browse to Down -
loads folder and move file and rename if desired. Do not change the file extension of the backup. It must be a .zip file. Do not
open the backup and then rezip using Windows. The backup can be corrupted by Windows.
390
Rev AC

TCS+
For instructions on how to load the backup to laser, see "Firmware (CMark) Update" on page 386 .
TCL Script
TCL scripts are files used to add specific additional functionality to the laser system. SAR files are delivered as TCL Scripts.
The following rules apply for TCL scripts to be uploaded:
l
The TCL script to be uploaded to the laser must be in a .zip file format and cannot be any other file type.
l
TCL scripts must also have the startup.sh file located at the root level within the .zip archive. If the startup.sh file is within a
folder inside the .zip archive then the TCL script will not run.
l
Only one TCL script can be uploaded. Loading a second TCL script will overwrite the previous one.
l
If two TCL scripts need to be loaded to the laser, the SAR group must combine them into one package.
Upload
1. Connect and log in to TCS+ as a User with access rights to upload a TCL script.
2. If using a TU4x0 touchscreen, insert into the TU4x0 USB port a USB drive containing the desired TCL script in the proper
format. The file must be in a folder called TCS on the USB drive if using a TU4x0. If using a web browser on a PC, then it is
not necessary to use a USB memory drive.
3. Press the Main Menu button.
4. Select File Management from the dropdown menu.
5. Press the Upload button at the top of the File Management window. It is not necessary to select TCL scripts from the list
first. When you select the file from the Windows Explorer window, the software will put the file into the correct file location
based on the file type you upload.
391
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
TCL Scripts must be formatted correctly for upload
Formatting Requirements
l
The file must have .zip file extension. It can have any file name, but needs to be .zip file.
l
There must be a startup.sh file at the root level of the .zip.
l
Only one TCL Script can be loaded. If multiple TCL scripts are required they must be combined by the
SAR group into one .zip. Loading a second TCL Script will overwrite the first TCL Script.
6. Select the TCL script for to be uploaded from Windows Explorer if using a web browser or from the USB drive if using a
TU4x0. The upload should not take more than a few seconds.
7. Press the OK button to acknowledge the dialogue box that notes a reboot is required.
8. Reboot the laser system to finish installation of TCL Script. Script is active after reboot.

TCL scripts to be uploaded to the laser must be in the correct format. Following are some of the items necessary for a package
that can be uploaded to the laser system through the Web Interface.

lowing figure shows the TCL files and folders used to run Ultimate 6 tcl as an example. There should be no subdirectories
between the .zip being loaded and the startup.sh file within that .zip.

ectories above the actual TCL package are present.

Options & Functions
l
Load Barcode Scan
For manufacturing only. Loads laser configuration from work order.
l
Reset CP Light
When a CP- Light is installed in a laser system a code is written to the CP- Light upon first boot that indicates the model,
wattage, umbilical length, and marking unit type. Once this code is written to the CP- Light the board can only be used in
that laser configuration. If CP- Light and marking unit are to be tested or used in a laser system with different umbilical
length or wattage then the CP- Light code must be reset or errors will occur. This button can only be accessed by Service
user.
l
Serial Number
Enter the serial number of the laser here. The serial number is found on a sticker located on the supply unit rear panel.
Only the Service user can change/set the laser type.
l
Name
Optional, unless using Videojet Remote Service (VRS). When using VRS, this is where the Friendly Name for the laser is
entered.
398
Rev AC

TCS+
l
Laser Type
Selection must match the actual laser type as well as correct umbilical length for 7440, or errors will assert in the user inter -
face. Only Service user can change/set the laser type.
l
Marking Head Type
Selection must match the marking head type in use or errors will assert in the user interface. Only the Service user can
change/set the laser type.
l
Tuning
Routine used for galvo control. Selections are either Quality (default) or Speed. High print speed text applications may be
better with Speed selected.
l
Lens
This value should be set to the currently installed lens.
Automatically selected with 7340/7440 based on marking head type selected. You cannot change the lens type inde -
pendently as lenses are glued into interior of marking unit.
l
Tube Length
Always set to 0 unless otherwise directed by authorized Videojet engineering or technical support. By default, only Service
can change this setting, but it is possible for the Service user to hand over Access Right to this setting to Admin or other
user.
l
Working Distance
Set by manufacturing, this is the optimal distance for the specific laser system and marking head installed as measured
from marking head output to the product. This setting cannot be adjusted in the TCS+ software by any User and the best
working distance may vary based on the material being marked and the effect desired. The Working Distance is saved in
the CP- Light during pairing of marking unit and CP- Light. Never separate a CP- Light and marking unit set.
l
Safety Circuit
Select the type of safety circuit used in the system from the dropdown selector. An incorrect setting here could cause
errors or inoperable laser until selection is corrected. Only Service user can change/set the laser type.
l
Leakage Protection Enabled
Always toggled ON. When ON, the shutter will close after 9 seconds of no product movement. The shutter will reopen
again when product starts to move. This feature prevents fires due to beam leakage when the product is stopped below
marking head.
l
Trigger enable voltage needed
This may be set to True if the application dictates that both Trigger and Trigger enable must be present to print.
Set to Default (7340/7440) - False. Default setting means that X25.9 does not require 24V in order to acknowledge Trig -
ger.
l
Disable Trigger/Encoder detached errors
Only available since CMark 3.4. Enable this if using trigger or encoder signals, but not using 24V output from X25 con -
nector to power trigger/encoder.
399
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
l
System Integration Mode
Changes the behavior of the System Ready output. Not recommended to change on most Videojet laser models. Leave
this set to Stand Alone Laser . By default only Service can change this setting, but it is possible for the Service user to
hand over Access Right to this setting to Admin or other user.
l
Simmer
This control is no longer supported, though it may be displayed. Changes cannot be made by the user.
Controls first pulse behavior (7340/7440 only).
Change Galvo Tuning
Some Videojet lasers have the ability to change between a Quality and Speed galvo tunings that are saved into the system. Qual -
ity tuning is the default setting is should be used in most cases. Speed can be selected where high speed marking with text char -
acters is needed. Speed setting should not be used with filled fonts or logos as some ripple in the lines used to fill in images will be
noticeable.
The following instructions only apply to lasers that have multiple tunings available (including 7230/7330/7340/7440) and which
have CMark 4.7 or newer installed.

Network Settings
Chameleon- based lasers have two Ethernet ports that can be configured with unique IP addresses through the Laser Unit Set -
tings within the TCS+ user interface. The two Ethernet ports on the Chameleon act as a switch and the IPs assigned are not spe -
cific to the upper or lower Ethernet port.
Configuring IP Address (es)
Configured IP Address (es) only take effect when Dip Switch 1 on Chameleon board is set to Off and the laser is rebooted after
the change. If the IP address of the laser is unknown, setting Dip Switch 1 on the Chameleon board to ON and rebooting will use
a known IP address to connect. After connection, you may connect and edit the IP address to a desired, unique IP or may dis -
connect or view the configured IP and then use the configured IP in the future. Make sure to set Dip Switch 1 back to the Off pos -
ition and reboot after either discovering the configured IP or after setting a new unique IP address.
If using the TU440/TU430 touchscreen to connect, no IP address is needed because the TU440/TU430 connects via specialized
HDMI.
1. Login as Service User or as a User who has been granted Access Rights to Network Settings within Laser Unit Settings..
2. Press the Main Menu button and select Laser Unit Settings from the dropdown menu.
403
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
3. Press the Network Settings option in the left column of the window displayed. It may be necessary to scroll down to
this option.
4. Enter the Primary IP address desired and the Subnet mask.
5. Enter a Secondary IP address and Subnet mask if desired.
6. Enter a Gateway if needed for the network.
7. Press the Save button.
8. Press the Close button.
9. Shut down the laser and, if necessary, move Dip Switch 1 to the Off position.
10. Reboot the laser for the changes to take effect. IP address changes take effect on boot up completion.
Permissions
Any computer on the same network and subnet as the laser can communicate with the laser system, however you must register a
PC's IP address to the laser to allow fire commands from that PC to the laser when using the SmartGraph user interface. IP
addresses can be added to the list with Permissions through TCS+ on lasers with CMark 4.7 or newer.
Adding an IP address to the laser's Permission may be necessary when:
l
Servicing a printer - register your laptop
l
Installing a laser system - register the PC or touchscreen computer that will control laser
404
Rev AC

TCS+
Registering Permissions
1. Login to TCS+.
2. At the Home screen, select Laser Unit Settings from the Main menu.
3. Select Permissions in the left column on the Laser Unit Settings screen. The IP addresses that have Permissions on the
laser system will be shown in the right column.
4. Click Add to enter an additional IP address. The system will display a dialogue box with the IP Address of the device you
are connected with. Edit the IP Address if that is not the desired IP Address for which to add permissions.
405
Rev AC

7340 7440 Technician Training Guide
5. Press OK . The list of IP addresses with permissions will display and the one just entered should appear in the list.
